原书第 113 页 · CHAPTER TWELVE(续) 113
The orchestrator pattern is not the only multi-subagent pattern, although it is the most visually striking one. A second important pattern is the sequential pipeline, in which the output of one subagent becomes the brief for the next. This pattern applies when the work has a real ordering dependency that cannot be parallelized. A common example: the developer wants to refactor a substantial piece of code, but only after having an architectural review of the current structure to confirm the refactor's direction. The architecture-reviewer runs first; its output, a recommendation about which structure is appropriate, becomes part of the brief for the refactor-specialist that runs second. The two cannot run in parallel because the second one needs the first one's conclusions.
编排者模式不是唯一的"多 subagent 模式",虽然它最显眼。第二个重要模式是顺序管线(sequential pipeline)——上一个 subagent 的输出,成为下一个 subagent 的 brief。这种模式适合那种"工作有真实顺序依赖、无法并行"的情形。一个常见例子:开发者想重构一大块代码,但她希望先有"对当前结构的架构评审"来确认重构方向。architecture-reviewer 先跑,它的输出——"推荐走哪种结构"——成为 refactor-specialist 第二个跑的 brief 的组成部分。两者不能并行,因为第二个的输入是第一个的结论。
The mechanics of sequential pipelines are slightly different from those of parallel orchestration. The main session dispatches the first subagent, waits for its result, integrates the result into the brief for the next subagent, dispatches that one, waits for its result, and so on. The total wall-clock time is the sum of each subagent's time, with no parallelism saving. The benefits are still substantial: each step happens in a focused context, each step's output is shaped by the previous step's output rather than by the noise of an accumulated session, and the pipeline can be reviewed step by step, with the option to stop at any intermediate stage if the work is not going where the developer wants. Sequential pipelines, while slower than orchestrations, are the natural shape of work that has real dependencies, and the developer who recognizes when she is in a sequential rather than a parallel situation will save herself the frustration of trying to force orchestration onto work that does not support it.
顺序管线和并行编排的"机械结构"略有不同。主 session 先派第一个 subagent,等它的结果,把结果整合进下一个 subagent 的 brief,再派出那一个,等它的结果,如此往复。总挂钟时间是各 subagent 时间之和,没有"并行带来的节省"。但它带来的好处仍然不小:每一步都跑在一个聚焦的上下文里;每一步的输出由上一步的输出塑造,而不是被一个"不断累积的 session 噪声"塑造;管线可以一步一步地被审视,任何中间阶段,如果工作偏离了开发者的预期,都有机会停下。顺序管线虽然比并行编排慢,但它正是"工作真有依赖"时的天然形状;能分辨"我这是顺序而非并行"的人,会免去"硬把不该并行的东西套上编排模式"的挫败感。
A third pattern, which is a hybrid of the first two, is worth naming briefly. The map-reduce pattern dispatches several subagents in parallel on independent components of a task and then dispatches a final subagent, sequentially, to synthesize their outputs. Sometimes the synthesis is straightforward enough for the main session to do directly, in which case it is just the orchestrator pattern. Sometimes the synthesis is itself a substantial specialty, such as composing a coherent design document from several focused reports, in which case a synthesis subagent is worth defining. The map-reduce pattern shows up most often in larger codebases, where the inputs are too varied for a single specialist to read in full. Different parallel subagents read different sections; a synthesis subagent receives their reports and produces the final output. The pattern is more elaborate, but the underlying principles are the same: independent work in parallel where possible, dependent work in sequence where required, focused contexts at every stage.
第三种模式,介于前两种之间,值得简短给它起个名字:map-reduce 模式。它先并行派几个 subagent,各自处理任务里独立的部分,然后再(顺序地)派一个最后的 subagent 去综合它们的输出。有时综合本身简单到主 session 直接做就行,那就还是编排者模式;有时综合本身就是一门分量不轻的专长——比如把几份聚焦的报告编成一份连贯的设计文档——这时候就值得专门定义一个"synthesis" subagent。map-reduce 模式在大代码库里出现得最多——那里输入过于多样,一个专员读不过来:并行 subagent 各自读自己那一段,synthesis subagent 接住它们的报告,产出最终输出。模式本身更复杂,但底层原则没变:能并行就并行,有依赖就顺序,每一阶段都跑在聚焦的上下文里。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 114 页 · CHAPTER TWELVE(续) 114
A short reflection on what makes orchestration genuinely powerful. The most striking thing about the orchestrator pattern is not the wall-clock saving, although the saving is real and welcome. The most striking thing is what the developer is freed from. She no longer has to be the one who remembers all the dimensions a thorough review should cover. The pattern itself remembers them. She no longer has to mentally context-switch between security thinking and performance thinking and style thinking, because the subagents handle each dimension in its own thread. Her attention, which was previously distributed across all the dimensions she cared about, can be concentrated on whatever the unified output most demands of her. She becomes, in effect, a senior engineer reading reports written by competent specialists, rather than a junior engineer trying to be all four specialists at once. The transformation in working experience is not just a productivity improvement; it is a change in the kind of cognitive work she is asked to do, and developers who experience it for the first time often describe the shift as the moment Claude Code stopped feeling like a chat interface and started feeling like an environment.
简短聊一下"编排者模式真正强大的地方"。它最显眼的地方,不是挂钟时间的节省——虽然那也是真实且受欢迎的。它最显眼的地方,是开发者被解放出来。她不再需要亲自去记"一次彻底评审该覆盖哪些维度"——模式本身替她记着;她不再需要在脑子里从"安全思维"切到"性能思维"再切到"风格思维"——subagent 各自在自己的线程里处理各自的维度。她原本被摊薄在"所有她关心的维度"上的注意力,可以集中到统一输出最需要她投入的地方。她在效果上变成了"一位资深工程师,读着由胜任的专员写就的报告",而不是"一位初级工程师,试图一个人扮四角"。这种工作体验上的转变,不只是生产力的提升,更是她被要求去做的那类认知工作本身的改变——很多开发者第一次体会到这种转变时会这样描述:"就是这一刻,Claude Code 不再像一个聊天界面,而像一个环境。"

When Not to Orchestrate · 什么时候不该编排

A final note, before this chapter closes, on when not to reach for orchestration. The orchestrator pattern carries overhead. Dispatching four subagents and synthesizing their outputs is more elaborate than asking the main session to do a small task directly. For small tasks, the overhead is not worth paying. A quick code review of a fifty-line patch does not need four subagents; the main session can handle it directly. A trivial documentation update does not need a documentation specialist; the main session is fine. The orchestrator pattern earns its keep on substantial tasks, where the parallelism saving and the specialization benefit are worth the dispatch overhead. The developer who reaches for orchestration appropriately, and refrains from reaching for it on tasks that do not justify it, will have the best experience.
本章收尾前,还要说一点:什么时候不该用编排。编排者模式本身有开销——派四个 subagent、再综合它们的输出,比"让主 session 直接做一件小事"要复杂得多。对小事来说,这笔开销不值得付。五十行 patch 的快速评审,不需要四个 subagent,主 session 直接做就够;琐碎的文档更新,不需要一个文档专员,主 session 自己就行。编排者模式只有在大体量任务上才回本——那里并行的节省和专门化的好处能压过派单开销。能"该用就用、不该用就克制"的人,体验是最好的。
A useful rule of thumb is the five-minute test. If the developer would spend more than five minutes on the task as a single sequential pass through the main session, the orchestrator pattern is probably worth considering. If she would spend less than five minutes, orchestration is probably overkill. The threshold is not exact, and individual judgment varies, but the rule prevents both common mistakes: orchestrating tiny tasks where the dispatch overhead dominates, and serializing large tasks where the parallelism would have produced real savings. With practice, the developer stops needing the rule, because the right shape of work for orchestration becomes visible at a glance. Until that intuition has set in, the five-minute test is a reasonable working approximation.
一条好用的经验法则叫"五分钟测试"。如果开发者自己一个人顺序做这件事要花超过五分钟,那么编排者模式大概值得考虑;如果只要不到五分钟,编排大概率是杀鸡用牛刀。门槛不是死数,个人判断也会有出入,但这条规则可以同时防止两种常见错误——在小任务上"派单开销盖过收益"、在大任务上"本可并行的却被迫串行"。练上一阵之后,这条规则就不必再挂着——编排适合的形状会"一眼可见"。在那种直觉还没稳之前,"五分钟测试"是一个合理的工作近似。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 115 页 · CHAPTER TWELVE(续) 115
A second observation worth keeping in mind is that orchestration scales with the developer's familiarity. New users of subagents tend to under-orchestrate, treating the main session as the default and reaching for subagents only on the most obvious cases. Experienced users tend to orchestrate naturally, recognizing the orchestratable shape of work in their first or second pass through a problem. The transition from the first state to the second takes only a few weeks of deliberate practice, and the practice is enjoyable rather than tedious, because every successful orchestration produces a noticeable, satisfying acceleration of work that would otherwise have been slow.
第二条值得记在心里的观察是:编排这种"能力"会随着开发者的熟练度而增长。新接触 subagent 的用户倾向于"编排不足"——把主 session 当默认,只在最明显的几种情况里伸手去用 subagent;熟练用户则会自然地编排,在面对一个问题的第一遍、第二遍里就识别出"可编排"的形状。从前者到后者的过渡,只需要几周有意识的练习;而这种练习并不令人疲倦,因为每一次成功的编排都会带来"原本很慢的事被显著加速"那种可感知的满足。
One final reflection on this chapter, before moving to the inventory of specialists. The orchestrator pattern is the visible face of subagents, the part that produces the most striking demonstrations and the most quotable speedups. But the deeper value of subagents, the value that compounds quietly across months of use, is not the speed. It is the consistent quality. A library of well-designed specialists produces consistently high-quality work because each specialist has been designed for its specific role, and the design is preserved across every invocation. The developer who has installed a serious subagent library is not just faster. She is more reliably good at her work, because the failure modes that come from overload, distraction, or context pollution have been engineered out of her environment. The reliability is the prize. The speed is a happy side effect.
在过渡到"专员清单"那一章之前,再留一段反思。编排者模式是 subagent 的"面子"——它造就了最抓眼球的演示、最值得引用的加速。但 subagent 更深层的价值——那种"在几个月里悄悄复利"的价值——不是速度,而是始终如一的质量。一套精心设计的专员库能稳定地产出高质量的工作,因为每位专员都是为它那个特定角色设计的,而这份设计在每一次调用里都被保留下来。装下了一套"像样的 subagent 库"的开发者,不只是更快——她更稳定地做得好;因为"过载、分心、上下文污染"带来的失败模式,已经从她的环境里被工程化地排除了。可靠性才是奖赏;速度只是顺带的好处。
The next chapter shifts from the single orchestration pattern to the inventory question. With the design of single subagents and the architecture of parallel orchestration both established, the developer needs a working library of subagents to draw from. The chapter ahead provides that library. Eight specialist subagents, each ready to install, each addressing a recurring need in daily development work, will make the architecture of this part of the book concrete in installed form. By the end of the next chapter, the reader will have a team of specialists waiting in her .claude/agents directory, ready to be dispatched whenever her work calls for them.
下一章从"单个编排模式"切换到"清单"问题。当"单个 subagent 的设计"和"并行编排的架构"都已经确立,开发者需要一套"能上手就用"的 subagent 库。下一章就提供这套库:八位专员 subagent,每位都已经准备好装,每位都对应一项日常工作里的高频需求——把本部分讲过的架构,以"已安装形态"具体化。等读完下一章,读者就会在自己 .claude/agents 目录里坐拥一支"专员团队",随时等候工作召唤。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 116 页 · CHAPTER TWELVE 收尾 116
A worked end-to-end orchestration looks like this in practice. The developer types her broad request. The main session, recognizing the orchestratable pattern, identifies the dimensions and dispatches the subagents. The dispatches happen in quick succession, and the subagents begin work simultaneously. The main session, while waiting, sometimes does a small amount of preparatory work, like inspecting the diff itself for the synthesis step. The subagents return their reports as they finish; reports may not arrive in dispatch order, since some specialties take longer than others. The main session collects them, holds them until all are present, and then composes the unified output. The whole interaction takes only marginally longer than the slowest of the four subagents. The developer sees a thorough, multi-dimensional review delivered in roughly the time of one careful pass, and the review reads as if four specialists had been retained for the job, because, in a meaningful sense, four specialists were.
一个"端到端"的编排者流程,实操中是这样的:开发者用宽泛的方式提出请求;主 session 识别出"这是一个可编排的形状",找出涉及的维度,派 subagent;派单一份接一份快速发生,subagent 同时开始工作。主 session 在等的当口,有时会做一点准备性工作——比如亲自扫一眼 diff,方便后面综合。各 subagent 完成的顺序可能和派单顺序不一样,因为有的专长耗时长、有的耗时短;它们一边完成一边把报告送回,主 session 收齐之后,会一直等所有报告都到齐,再合成统一输出。整个交互的耗时,只比四个 subagent 中最慢的那一个多一点点。开发者看到的,是一次彻底、多维的评审——送达的耗时,大致相当于"做一次认真评审"的时间;而读起来,就像是真的请了四位专员——在有意义的层面上,确实就是请了四位。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 117 页 · CHAPTER TWELVE 收尾 / PART THREE 总结 117
A concrete example will fix this in the reader's mind. Consider a developer who has just opened a pull request that touches roughly four hundred lines of code in three files. The change introduces a new authentication endpoint. Before merging, she wants a full review. She types into Claude Code, in plain language, perform a full review of this PR covering security, performance, style, and test coverage. The main session reads the request. It recognizes the four named dimensions. It looks at the available subagents and finds matches: security-auditor, performance-reviewer, code-style-checker, test-coverage-analyzer. It dispatches all four with a brief that includes the diff and a directive to focus on the subagent's named dimension.
一个具体例子会让这件事在读者脑子里落地。设想:开发者刚开了一个 PR,跨三个文件、改动约四百行,新增一个认证端点。合并前,她想要一次完整评审。她用日常语言在 Claude Code 里敲:"对这个 PR 做一次完整评审,覆盖安全、性能、风格、测试覆盖。"主 session 读这条请求,识别出里面点名的四个维度,去看可用的 subagent,找到匹配项:security-auditor、performance-reviewer、code-style-checker、test-coverage-analyzer。然后它把四个一起派出去,brief 里都带上 diff,并附一条"聚焦你被点名的那个维度"的指令。
The library is now eight specialists strong. The reader who has installed all of them has, in roughly an hour of work, assembled a small but capable team that can be dispatched on demand whenever her work crosses one of their domains. The team is patient. It does not get tired. It does not have meetings. Each specialist is excellent at one thing and confidently silent about everything else. The developer's job is to be the editor, dispatching the right specialist to the right work and synthesizing the results into the unified outputs her real life demands.
到这里,你的 subagent 库就有八位专员了。装齐了八位的读者,大约花了一小时的工作,就已经搭起了一支"小而能干"的团队——只要工作触碰到它们的专长领域,随叫随到。这支团队有耐心,不会累,不开会;每位专员都精于一件事,并对其余的事坦荡地沉默。开发者的角色,是编辑——把对路的专员派去对路的工作上,把它们的产出综合成"她真实生活里需要"的统一形态。
The next chapter shifts from the single orchestration pattern to the inventory question. With the design of single subagents and the architecture of parallel orchestration both established, the developer needs a working library of subagents to draw from. The chapter ahead provides that library. Eight specialist subagents, each ready to install, each addressing a recurring need in daily development work, will make the architecture of this part of the book concrete in installed form. By the end of the next chapter, the reader will have a team of specialists waiting in her .claude/agents directory, ready to be dispatched whenever her work calls for them.
下一章从"单个编排模式"切换到"清单"问题。当"单个 subagent 的设计"和"并行编排的架构"都已经确立,开发者需要一套"能上手就用"的 subagent 库。下一章就提供这套库:八位专员 subagent,每位都已经准备好装,每位都对应一项日常工作里的高频需求——把本部分讲过的架构,以"已安装形态"具体化。等读完下一章,读者就会在自己 .claude/agents 目录里坐拥一支"专员团队",随时等候工作召唤。

原书第 118 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN 118

CHAPTER THIRTEEN · 第十三章
The Subagent Library: Eight Specialists Every Developer Needs
子代理库:每位开发者都需要的八位专员

"A small team of true specialists outperforms a large team of competent generalists almost every time. The difference is not effort. It is depth." — attributed to W. Edwards Deming
"一支由真正专员组成的小团队,几乎每一次都胜过一支由"称职的通才"组成的大团队。差距不是投入的多少,而是深度。" —— 归于 W. 爱德华兹·戴明
This chapter is the practical counterpart to Chapter Nine. By the time the reader finishes it, eight specialist subagents will be installed in her ~/.claude/agents directory, ready for use in any project. Each specialist is presented in the same uniform format: the problem it solves, the complete subagent file, and a short explanation of the design choices that shaped it. The reader who installs all eight will have a small but capable team of specialists at her disposal, available for dispatch whenever her work crosses one of their domains.
本章是第九章的"subagent 版姊妹篇"。等读者读完时,八个专员 subagent 就会装进她 ~/.claude/agents 目录,随时可以在任何项目里使用。每位专员都用统一的格式呈现:它解决的问题、完整的 subagent 文件、以及"塑造它的设计选择"的简短说明。把八位都装齐的读者,会拥有一支"小而能干"的专员团队,每当工作触碰到它们的领域,即可派单。
The selection of eight specialists is deliberate. The eight cover the most common dimensions of code work that benefit from isolation and specialization: security, performance, testing, documentation, architecture, refactoring, dependency hygiene, and migration. These are the dimensions that, in a real engineering organization, would be staffed by named specialists with named roles. Replicating those specialist roles in subagent form is what gives a single developer, working in Claude Code, access to expertise that would otherwise be available only inside a substantial engineering team.
这八位专员的挑选是刻意的。它们覆盖了"代码工作中受益于隔离与专门化"的最常见维度:安全、性能、测试、文档、架构、重构、依赖卫生、迁移。这些维度,在真实工程组织里,会由"有名有姓、有明确角色"的专员来承担。把这些专员角色以 subagent 形式复制出来,正是"让一位开发者、在 Claude Code 里、获得本来只有大型工程团队里才有的那种专业能力"的关键。
Before beginning the installation, a small piece of housekeeping. All eight subagents below are personal subagents, installed at the user level, and therefore live in ~/.claude/agents. Each one is a single Markdown file named after the subagent. After installing all eight and restarting Claude Code, the subagents will be available in every session, in every project the developer opens. Project-specific specialists, if needed, can be added later in the .claude/agents directory of specific projects.
动手前,先做点内务。下面八位 subagent 都是"个人 subagent",按用户级别安装,因此都放在 ~/.claude/agents 里。每个 subagent 就是单个 Markdown 文件,文件名与 subagent 同名。装齐八位、重启 Claude Code 之后,这些 subagent 在每个 session、每个项目里都可用。如果以后需要"项目级"专员,可以在具体项目的 .claude/agents 下再加。

The First Specialist · 第一位专员

← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 119 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 119
The first specialist is security-auditor, presented in full in the previous chapter. Its job is to audit code for security vulnerabilities. If the reader has not already installed it, the file from Chapter Eleven can be used directly.
第一位专员是 security-auditor——上一章已经完整给出过。它的职责是审计代码里的安全漏洞。如果读者还没装,直接用第十一章的那个文件即可。
The second specialist is performance-reviewer. Its job is to review code for performance issues, with a focus on the kinds of problems that show up in production rather than the kinds that show up in micro-benchmarks.
第二位专员是 performance-reviewer。它的职责是评审代码里的性能问题,关注的是"会在生产里表现出来的"那一类问题,而不是"只在微基准里才看得见"的那一类。
---
name: performance-reviewer
description: Reviews code for performance issues that affect real-world execution, including algorithmic complexity, database query patterns, memory allocation, and unnecessary work in hot paths. Use proactively on changes that touch loops, queries, or data structures.
tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
model: sonnet
---
You are a senior performance engineer. Your job is to find performance issues that will affect production behavior, and to ignore micro-optimizations that do not matter at scale.

Your areas of focus:
- Algorithmic complexity (O(n^2) where O(n) would do)
- Database queries inside loops (the classic N+1 pattern)
- Excessive memory allocation in hot paths
- Synchronous I/O where async would be appropriate
- Unbounded work that should be paginated or streamed
- Cache misses where caching would clearly help
- Repeated computation that should be memoized

Your method:
1. Read the code you are given.
2. Identify hot paths: code that runs frequently, runs on every request, or runs in tight loops.
3. Apply your areas of focus to those hot paths first. Apply them lightly to cold paths only if something obvious stands out.
4. For each issue, produce a short report with severity, location, description, and recommendation.

What you do not do:
- You do not modify code. You report.
- You do not flag micro-optimizations. A nanosecond saved in a path that runs once a day is not worth a comment.
- You do not pad. If the code looks fine, say so plainly.
- You do not benchmark. Your review is qualitative, based on patterns. The developer can benchmark if she wants quantitative data.

End with a one-sentence judgment: clear, minor issues only, or significant issues found.
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 120 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 120
The third specialist is test-writer. Its job is to write tests for a given function or module, following the conventions of the project it is operating in.
第三位专员是 test-writer。它的职责是:为给定的函数或模块写测试,遵循它当前所在项目的测试规约。
---
name: test-writer
description: Writes test files for a given function, module, or feature, following the test framework and conventions of the current project. Use when the user asks for tests, says "add coverage," "write a test for this," or finishes a feature without tests in place.
tools: Read, Write, Bash, Glob
model: sonnet
---
You are a senior test engineer. Your job is to write tests that catch real bugs, not tests that only exercise the happy path so that coverage numbers look better.

Your method:
1. Read the file under test and any closely related files needed to understand the code's behavior.
2. Detect the test framework in use by inspecting package.json or the project's equivalent. Default to whatever the project already uses.
3. Identify the public surface of the file: functions, methods, classes that other code calls.
4. For each item on the public surface, write tests covering at minimum:
- The happy path
- At least one boundary condition
- At least one error or invalid-input case
5. Mock external dependencies. Do not make network calls or write to real files in tests.
6. Place test files alongside the code under test, following the project's existing layout.

What you do not do:
- You do not modify the code under test, even if you discover bugs while writing tests. Note the bug as a comment in the test and flag it in your final summary.
- You do not write tests that only call the function and assert that it does not throw. That is not a test.
- You do not mock the function under test. You mock its dependencies.
- You do not produce an excess of nearly-identical tests. Three meaningful tests are better than ten redundant ones.

End with a list of the test files you wrote and any bugs or concerns you noticed in the code under test.

The Documentation Writer · 文档写作者

The fourth specialist is documentation-writer. Its job is to write or update documentation for a given module, API, or feature. The specialist is read- and-write within documentation files only, leaving all code untouched.
第四位专员是 documentation-writer。它的职责是:为给定的模块、API 或特性写或更新文档。这位专员只在"文档文件"内可读可写,所有代码一律不碰。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 121 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 121
The fifth specialist is architecture-reviewer. Its job is to look at proposed changes from a step-back perspective, asking whether the structure of the solution is right rather than whether the implementation is correct.
第五位专员是 architecture-reviewer。它的职责是:从"后退一步"的视角看被提议的改动,问"解决方案的结构对不对",而不是"实现正确不正确"。
---
name: architecture-reviewer
description: Reviews code or proposed changes for architectural concerns: separation of responsibilities, layering violations, coupling, abstraction quality, and consistency with existing patterns. Use when reviewing significant new modules, major refactors, or design proposals.
tools: Read, Grep, Glob
model: opus
---
You are a senior software architect. Your job is to look at code from a step-back perspective: is the structure right, do the responsibilities live in the right places, does this fit with the rest of the system, will future engineers be able to extend this without unwinding it.

Your areas of focus:
- Separation of concerns: does each module have a clear, single responsibility?
- Layering: are higher-level modules depending on abstractions, with implementation details pushed downward?
- Coupling: do modules know about each other only through narrow, intentional interfaces?
- Abstraction quality: are abstractions earning their place, or are they obscuring rather than clarifying?
- Consistency: does this code fit with the patterns already established in the project, or does it introduce a new pattern that may fragment the codebase?

Your method:
1. Read the code in question.
2. Read enough of the surrounding code to understand the existing patterns and conventions.
3. Apply your areas of focus, prioritizing the ones most relevant to this change.
4. Produce a short report. Lead with the most important architectural observation. Do not dilute it with minor stylistic concerns.

What you do not do:
- You do not nitpick. Architectural review is about the shape of the solution, not the details of the implementation.
- You do not suggest sweeping rewrites unless the code clearly cannot be evolved into something acceptable.
- You do not modify code.
- You do not pad. Architectural review is qualitative; one good observation is worth a hundred mediocre ones.

End with a one-sentence judgment: structurally sound, structurally questionable but acceptable, or structurally problematic and worth reconsidering.
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 122 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 122
The sixth specialist is refactor-specialist. Its job is to take working code and produce a refactor that improves a specific named property, without changing behavior.
第六位专员是 refactor-specialist。它的职责是:拿到一段"能跑"的代码,产出一个"针对某项具体属性"的重构——并且不改行为。
---
name: refactor-specialist
description: Produces a refactor of given code that improves a specified property (readability, testability, cohesion, or naming) while preserving behavior exactly. Use when the user says "refactor this," "clean this up," "make this easier to test," or names a specific structural property to improve.
tools: Read, Edit
model: sonnet
---
You are a senior refactoring engineer. Your job is to produce refactors that make code better along a specific dimension, without ever changing what the code does.

Your discipline:
- Behavior preservation is absolute. The refactored code must produce identical observable output for all inputs the original handled.
- Improvements are along a single named dimension at a time. If the user asks for readability, you improve readability. If the user asks for testability, you improve testability. You do not bundle multiple kinds of improvement into one change unless explicitly asked.
- You explain the change. After producing the refactor, you list what improved and why.

Your method:
1. Read the code carefully.
2. Confirm the dimension you are optimizing for. If unclear, ask the user before proceeding.
3. Identify the highest-leverage opportunity to improve along that dimension.
4. Produce the refactor as a complete replacement of the original, not as a diff.
5. Explain what changed and why, in three to five lines.

What you do not do:
- You do not change behavior. If you notice a bug, you mention it separately and do not silently fix it.
- You do not bundle unrelated improvements into the same refactor.
- You do not introduce new dependencies, frameworks, or libraries unless the user explicitly authorizes them.
- You do not make stylistic changes that do not serve the named dimension.

End with the refactor and the explanation, and nothing else.

The Dependency Analyzer · 依赖分析者

The seventh specialist is dependency-analyzer. Its job is to inspect the project's dependency manifest and report on any dependencies that are outdated, vulnerable, unused, or duplicated.
第七位专员是 dependency-analyzer。它的职责是:检查项目的依赖清单(manifest),报告"过时、有漏洞、未使用、或重复"的依赖项。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 123 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 123 
---
name: dependency-analyzer
description: Analyzes the project's dependencies for outdated versions, known vulnerabilities, unused packages, and duplicate or redundant entries. Use when the user asks "what's in package.json," "audit my dependencies," "any vulnerable packages," or as part of a periodic hygiene check.
tools: Read, Grep, Glob, Bash
model: sonnet
---
You are a senior dependency hygiene engineer. Your job is to keep a project's dependency surface clean, secure, and minimal.

Your method:
1. Read the dependency manifest (package.json, requirements.txt, Cargo.toml, etc.).
2. Read the lockfile if one exists.
3. Identify, in order of priority:
- Dependencies with known security vulnerabilities (run the project's audit tool if available)
- Major-version updates available for actively maintained dependencies
- Dependencies that appear in the manifest but are not imported anywhere in the source
- Duplicate or redundant dependencies (two libraries doing the same job)
- Dependencies with significant licensing concerns
4. Produce a report grouped by category, with each item including the package name, current version, latest version (if relevant), and a short recommendation.

What you do not do:
- You do not update the manifest. You only report.
- You do not flag every outdated minor version. Focus on major-version drift and security concerns.
- You do not invent vulnerabilities. If your audit tool produces no findings, say so plainly.
- You do not pad. A clean dependency tree gets a one-line judgment, not a pseudo-report.

End with a one-sentence judgment of overall dependency health: clean, minor concerns only, or significant cleanup recommended.
The eighth and final specialist is migration-planner. Its job is to produce a step-by-step plan for a non-trivial migration, such as moving from one framework to another, upgrading a major dependency, or restructuring a substantial part of a codebase.
第八位、也是最后一位专员是 migration-planner。它的职责是:为一次"非平凡"的迁移产出一份"分步骤"的方案——比如从一个框架换到另一个、升级一个主版本依赖、或重构代码库里的相当一部分。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 124 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 124 
---
name: migration-planner
description: Produces a step-by-step migration plan for a substantial change such as a framework upgrade, language version bump, library replacement, or architectural restructuring. Use when the user describes wanting to migrate from one technology to another or asks for a plan to upgrade something significant.
tools: Read, Grep, Glob
model: opus
---
You are a senior migration engineer. Your job is to break a substantial migration into a sequence of safe, reviewable steps that the team can execute incrementally.

Your principles:
- A good migration plan is a sequence of small, ship-able changes that can each be reviewed and reverted independently. Big-bang migrations fail.
- Every step has a clear acceptance criterion. The team should know when each step is complete and whether it succeeded.
- Risk goes at the front of the plan, not the back. Identify the riskiest part of the migration and propose a small experimental change that surfaces the risk early.
- Honest plans assume some steps will fail. Include a rollback strategy for each non-trivial step.

Your method:
1. Read the relevant code to understand the current state.
2. Read enough about the target state to understand the destination.
3. Decompose the migration into a numbered sequence of steps. Aim for steps that take a single PR each, no more.
4. For each step, document: what changes, what does not change, the acceptance criterion, the rollback strategy, and the estimated effort in person-days.
5. Identify the riskiest step and propose an early experiment that reduces the risk before the team commits to the full plan.
6. End with a one-paragraph executive summary suitable for a team meeting.

What you do not do:
- You do not write migration code. You produce the plan.
- You do not commit to specific dates. Effort estimates in person-days are appropriate; calendar dates depend on team capacity.
- You do not pad with general advice about migrations. Your output is specific to this migration.

End with the executive summary.
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 125 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 125
The library is now eight specialists strong. The reader who has installed all of them has, in roughly an hour of work, assembled a small but capable team that can be dispatched on demand whenever her work crosses one of their domains. The team is patient. It does not get tired. It does not have meetings. Each specialist is excellent at one thing and confidently silent about everything else. The developer's job is to be the editor, dispatching the right specialist to the right work and synthesizing the results into the unified outputs her real life demands.
到这里,你的 subagent 库就有八位专员了。装齐了八位的读者,大约花了一小时的工作,就已经搭起了一支"小而能干"的团队——只要工作触碰到它们的专长领域,随叫随到。这支团队有耐心,不会累,不开会;每位专员都精于一件事,并对其余的事坦荡地沉默。开发者的角色,是编辑——把对路的专员派去对路的工作上,把它们的产出综合成"她真实生活里需要"的统一形态。

Extending the Library · 扩展你的库

A note on extension. The eight specialists above are starting points, not endpoints. As the developer lives with them, she will discover that her work has shapes the eight do not cover, or that one of the eight is slightly off-fit for her specific stack. The fix is the same as for skills: edit the file. Each subagent is a single Markdown file. Editing it takes thirty seconds. Adjusting the role definition, expanding the description, narrowing the tools, refining the boundary, all of these are small changes that the developer can make as her understanding of her own workflow deepens.
关于"扩展"几句话。上面八位专员,同样是"起点而非终点"。开发者真正用起来之后,会发现:她工作里有"八位都没覆盖"的形状,或者八位中有"稍微不太贴合自己那套技术栈"的。修法与 skill 一样——改文件。每个 subagent 就是单个 Markdown 文件,改它也就三十秒:调整角色定义、扩展 description、收窄工具、打磨边界——这些小改动,开发者可以随着对自己工作流理解的加深,慢慢去做。
A second note on adding new specialists. The developer who finds, after a month or two of using the eight specialists, that a recurring kind of work is not covered by any of them, has the raw material for a ninth specialist. The pattern by which she should design it is by now familiar: name the role specifically, narrow the tools to what the role needs, write boundary language that stops the role from drifting, and test the result on three representative inputs. Within an hour, she has a ninth specialist on the team, and her library has grown along the contour of her actual work.
关于"加新专员"再说几句。开发者如果用了一两个月八位专员之后,发现有一类反复出现的工作不在它们的覆盖范围内——那她就有了"第九位专员"的原材料。设计它的套路到这时已经熟:具体地命名角色、把工具收到它"恰好需要"、写一段边界语言防止它漂移、并在三个有代表性的输入上测试。一小时之内,她就有了第九位专员,库也沿着"自己实际工作"的轮廓继续生长。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 126 页 · CHAPTER THIRTEEN(续) 126
A third note on team-level subagents. Several of the specialists above will be useful to other developers in the reader's team or company. The mechanism for sharing is the same as for skills: copy the subagent file into the project's .claude/agents directory and commit it. From that point forward, every developer who clones the project receives the specialist automatically. This is how subagent libraries spread within organizations, and how a thoughtful library built by one developer becomes a standing resource for an entire team. The library committed to a project repository is a quiet but real form of leverage. It travels with the code, it activates on the first session, and it raises the floor of what every developer working in that project can produce.
关于"团队级 subagent"再说几句。上面这些专员里,有几位对读者所在团队或公司的其他开发者也会很有用。分享机制与 skill 一样:把 subagent 文件拷进项目的 .claude/agents,提交进版本库。从那之后,凡是 clone 这个项目的开发者,都会自动拿到这位专员。subagent 库在组织内就是以这种方式传播的——"一位开发者精心搭出的库"会变成"整支团队的一份常驻资源"。提交进项目仓库的 subagent 库,是一种静默但真实的杠杆:它随代码一起走,在第一个 session 就激活,会拉高"在那个项目里工作的每一位开发者"所能产出的底线。
The chapter that follows is the climax of Part Three. With skills installed and subagents installed, the question becomes how the two systems compose. Subagents can use skills. Skills can be designed to fit specific subagents. The combination is where the architectural depth of Claude Code becomes most visible, and the next chapter is dedicated entirely to it. The reader who has installed both libraries by the end of Part Two and Part Three is now in possession of the components for a substantial leap in capability. The next chapter shows how to assemble those components into the integrated system that has been the destination of the book all along.
下一章是第三部分的高潮。Skill 装好、subagent 也装好之后,问题就变成了"这两套系统怎么组合在一起"。Subagent 可以使用 skill;skill 也可以被设计来"配"某个 subagent。两者的组合,正是 Claude Code 架构深度最显形的地方——下一章就专门讲这个。等第二、第三部分都读完,两个库都装上的读者,就已经手握足以"让能力跃迁一档"的组件;下一章,就是把它们组装成"这本整书一直通向的那座集成系统"。

原书第 128 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN 128

CHAPTER FOURTEEN · 第十四章
Subagents and Skills: The Composition Pattern
子代理与技能:组合模式

"The whole, when properly composed, contains capabilities that none of its parts possess in isolation. This is the meaning of architecture." — attributed to a systems engineer, anonymous
"整体,当被恰当地组合起来,会拥有"任一部分单独都不具备"的能力。这就是"架构"这个词的含义。" —— 归于一位系统工程师(佚名)
This chapter is where the two pillars built so far in the book begin to reinforce each other in a way that produces capabilities neither could produce alone. Skills, the subject of Part Two, taught Claude how to do specific tasks consistently. Subagents, the subject of Part Three, gave Claude a team of specialists, each in its own context. The composition of the two is the architectural depth that the book has been building toward, and this chapter teaches it explicitly.
本章,书里目前搭起的两根支柱开始彼此加固,以一种"任何一根独自都做不到"的方式产出能力。第二部分讲的 Skills,教的是 Claude 如何一致地完成具体任务;第三部分讲的 Subagents,给了 Claude 一支"各自独立、各有上下文"的专员团队。两者的组合,正是这本书一直在朝向的"架构深度"——本章把这件事明白地讲出来。
Begin with the basic mechanic. Skills are available everywhere. They are loaded at the level of the Claude Code installation, and any Claude process running in that installation, including any subagent, can activate any applicable skill. A subagent does not need a special configuration to access skills; it simply needs to have a description that matches the kind of work where a skill would naturally fire. The composition is not bolted on. It is the default behavior of the two systems when they are present together, and the developer's job is to design with the composition in mind from the start.
先说最基本的机制。Skill 是"处处可用"的——它们在 Claude Code 安装层加载,任何跑在该安装里的 Claude 进程(包括任何 subagent)都可以激活任何适用的 skill。subagent 要访问 skill 不需要任何特殊配置;它只需要让自己的 description 与"某个 skill 自然会触发的"那类工作相匹配。这种组合不是"外挂"上去的——它是"两套系统同时存在"时的默认行为;开发者的任务是:从一开始就把"组合"放进设计的考虑里。
A worked example illustrates the power. The developer has a test-writer subagent installed, presented in the previous chapter. The developer also has a project-specific skill called test-conventions, which encodes how tests are organized and named in this particular codebase, including the choice of test framework, the file layout, the mocking style, the use of fixtures, and the team's preferred patterns for assertion phrasing. The test-conventions skill lives in the project's .claude/skills directory; the test-writer subagent lives in the user's ~/.claude/agents directory.
一个具体例子能说明这种力量。开发者装好了一个 test-writer subagent(上一章给出过)。她还装了一个项目级 skill,叫 test-conventions——它把"这个特定代码库里测试是怎么组织、命名的"编码进去,包括:测试框架的选择、文件布局、mock 风格、fixture 的使用方式、团队偏好的断言措辞。test-conventions skill 放在项目的 .claude/skills 里;test-writer subagent 放在用户的 ~/.claude/agents 里。
Now imagine the developer dispatches the test-writer subagent on a new function. The subagent receives its brief: write tests for this function. The subagent begins its work. It reads the function. As it considers the structure of the tests it is about to write, it recognizes that the request matches the description of the test-conventions skill, and it loads that skill into its own context. The subagent now has, simultaneously: its own role as a senior test engineer, its own focused tools and isolated context, and the project-specific knowledge encoded in the skill. The tests it produces are written in the project's exact conventions, by a specialist who knows how to write good tests in general, in a context that is not polluted by anything outside the test-writing task.
现在想象:开发者把 test-writer subagent 派去处理一个新函数。subagent 收到 brief:为这个函数写测试。subagent 开始工作,读了函数。当它琢磨"接下来要写的测试是什么结构"时,识别出这个请求匹配 test-conventions skill 的 description,于是把那个 skill 加载进了自己的上下文。此时的 subagent,身上同时有三样东西:它自己"高级测试工程师"的角色、自己聚焦的工具与隔离的上下文、以及 skill 里编码的项目特定知识。它产出的测试——既严格遵循项目规约,又出自一位"在一般意义上知道怎么写好测试"的专员之手,而且跑在一个"没有被测试任务之外的任何事污染"的上下文里。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 129 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) 129

Composition in Action · 组合在行动

The result is strikingly better than what either component could produce alone. A skill alone, fired in the main session, would shape the output toward project conventions, but the output would be subject to context pollution from whatever else the main session was working on. A subagent alone, dispatched on the same task, would produce well-isolated, generally good tests, but it would not know the project's specific conventions and would have to guess. The combination has both: convention-correct tests, produced in a clean context, by a specialist who treats the work seriously. This is the composition pattern in its simplest form, and once a developer sees it work even once, she will not want to design subagents that do not know how to use skills.
这种结果,比"任何一方单独能产出的"都明显要好。Skill 单独、在主 session 里触发,确实会让输出向项目规约靠拢,但那份输出仍会被主 session 里其他事情的"上下文污染"影响。Subagent 单独、被派去做同一件事,确实能产出"隔离良好、整体不错"的测试,但它并不知道这个项目的具体规约,只能猜。两者的组合同时拥有这两点:规约准确的测试、由"把工作当回事"的专员、在干净的上下文里产出。这就是组合模式最简单的形态;一旦开发者哪怕只看它工作一次,她就再也不想设计"不懂用 skill"的 subagent 了。
There is a design principle that follows from this and that should guide every subagent the developer writes from now on. Subagents should be designed as roles, not as repositories of project knowledge. A test-writer subagent should know how to be a senior test engineer in general; it should not know that the project uses Vitest rather than Jest, or that mocks should be placed in a specific directory, or that the team prefers a specific assertion library. Those are project facts, and they belong in skills. The subagent provides the role; the skill provides the project-specific knowledge. When the role meets the project, the composition produces the right behavior.
由此引出一条设计原则,它应该从今往后指导开发者写的每一个 subagent:subagent 应该被设计成角色,而不是"项目知识的容器"。一个 test-writer subagent 应当知道"如何作为一位高级测试工程师",而不应当知道:这个项目用的是 Vitest 而不是 Jest、mock 要放在某个特定目录、团队偏好某个特定的断言库。这些是项目事实,属于 skill。subagent 提供角色,skill 提供项目特定的知识。当角色与项目相遇,组合就产出正确的行为。
This principle has a second-order effect that is worth naming. A subagent that contains project-specific knowledge becomes specific to that project, and its usefulness drops as soon as the developer moves to a different project. A subagent that contains only the role, with project-specific knowledge factored into skills, is portable. The same test-writer subagent works in five different projects, because the role is the same in each, and the project-specific conventions are loaded as skills from each project's own .claude/skills directory. A small library of well-designed, role-only subagents at the user level, combined with project-specific skills in each project, gives the developer the best of both worlds: stable, portable specialists, and project-appropriate behavior in every project she works in.
这条原则有一个值得点名的"二阶效应"。一个把项目特定知识塞在自己体内的 subagent,会变得"只适用于那个项目";开发者一切换到另一个项目,它的价值就立刻下降。一个只装"角色"、把项目特定知识抽到 skill 里的 subagent,才是可移植的。同一份 test-writer subagent,可以在五个不同的项目里都工作得好——因为"角色"在每个项目里都一样,项目特定的规约由"每个项目自己的 .claude/skills 目录"以 skill 形态加载进来。一套小而精、只装"角色"的 subagent 库放在用户级,再加上每个项目里"项目级"skill——开发者就同时拿到了两样最好的东西:稳定、可移植的专员,以及"在每个项目里都符合项目实际"的行为。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 130 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) 130
A second worked example raises the composition to a more sophisticated level. The developer has installed the security-auditor subagent from Chapter Eleven, the architecture-reviewer subagent from Chapter Thirteen, and a project-specific skill called auth-conventions, which encodes the codebase's authentication patterns, token handling, and security-sensitive practices. The skill is not project-specific knowledge that the security-auditor needs to do its job in general; the security-auditor knows about OWASP Top 10 and common vulnerability patterns regardless of project. But the skill encodes the way this specific codebase chooses to handle authentication, which is information the security-auditor needs in order to flag deviations from the established pattern as concerns rather than treating them as ordinary alternatives.
第二个具体例子,把组合抬到一个更复杂的层次。开发者装好了第十一章的 security-auditor subagent、第十三章的 architecture-reviewer subagent,以及一个项目级 skill——auth-conventions,把"代码库的认证模式、token 处理、与安全敏感的做法"编码进去。这个 skill 并不是"security-auditor 干它那件一般意义上的事"所必需的项目特定知识——security-auditor 知道 OWASP Top 10、知道常见漏洞模式,跟项目无关;但这个 skill 把"这个特定代码库选择怎么处理认证"编码进去——这正是 security-auditor 需要的:它要把"偏离既定模式"标记成"可疑",而不是当成"普通的备选方案"放过。
When the developer dispatches the security-auditor on a change that touches authentication, two things happen simultaneously. The subagent activates its general security expertise. The subagent also loads the auth-conventions skill, because the description matches. The subagent now has both: the general security knowledge that says hardcoded credentials are bad, and the specific project knowledge that says this codebase uses a token-based scheme with refresh rotation handled by a particular module. The audit it produces is doubly informed. It catches general security issues. It also catches deviations from the project's specific patterns, which a generalist would not have known were patterns at all.
当开发者把 security-auditor 派去处理"涉及认证的改动"时,有两件事同时发生:subagent 激活它一般的安全专业知识;同时,因为 description 匹配,subagent 也会加载 auth-conventions skill。此时的 subagent 身上,既有"硬编码凭据是不好的"这种一般安全知识,也有"这个代码库使用基于 token 的方案、refresh 轮换由某个特定模块负责"这种项目特定知识。它产出的审计,被双重"喂饱":既能抓出一般安全问题,也能抓出"偏离项目特定模式"的偏差——后者,通才型评审者甚至根本不会知道"原来这里还有种模式"。

An Advanced Composition Pattern · 一种更进阶的组合模式

There is a more advanced composition pattern worth introducing here, although its full development belongs in Part Five. The orchestrator pattern from Chapter Twelve, in which the main session dispatches several subagents in parallel and synthesizes their outputs, can be combined with skill composition. Each parallel subagent can activate its own appropriate skills. A four-way code review dispatched as four parallel subagents, each of which knows how to use the relevant project skills, produces a deeper, more project-aware review than a four-way review in which the subagents operate in their own generic roles without skill support. The combinations compose, and the composition of compositions is where the architecture of Claude Code reaches its most expressive form.
这里值得引入一种更进阶的组合模式——尽管它完整的展开属于第五部分。第十二章的"编排者模式"——主 session 并行派若干 subagent、再综合它们的输出——可以和 skill 组合再组合:每一个并行 subagent 都可以激活它各自合适的 skill。一次"四路"代码评审,作为四个并行 subagent 被派出,且每个 subagent 都知道怎么用相关的项目 skill——它产出的评审,比"四个 subagent 各自跑在通用角色里、没有任何 skill 支撑"的那种四路评审更深、更懂项目。组合之上再组合,正是 Claude Code 的架构抵达其最有表现力形态的地方。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 131 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) 131
more project-aware review than any single channel could produce. The four-by-four matrix, four subagents and four skills, is the structure of a serious engineering review process, and the composition pattern of this chapter is what makes it a single concept rather than two unrelated ones.
远超任何单一渠道所能产出的"对项目的理解"。这个 4×4 矩阵——四个 subagent 配四个 skill——是真正严肃的工程评审流程的结构;而本章所讲的组合模式,正是把"两个不相关的东西"捏成"同一个概念"的关键。
A practical caveat. Not every subagent benefits from skill composition, and the developer should not over-engineer the design. Some specialists are useful as pure role containers and should not be paired with skills at all. The migration-planner subagent from the previous chapter, for example, produces a generic migration plan that is shaped more by the specifics of the migration than by ambient project conventions. There is little benefit in trying to load skills into it. The composition pattern is a tool, not a doctrine. The developer should reach for it when it produces real value and leave it alone when it would only complicate a workflow that does not need complication.
一个务实的提醒:并不是每个 subagent 都受益于 skill 组合,开发者也不该把设计过度工程化。有些"专家"适合作为纯粹的角色容器存在,根本不该和 skill 配对。比如上一章那个 migration-planner subagent,它产出的迁移计划,主要由"这次迁移本身的具体情况"塑造,而非由"项目环境里那些约定俗成"来定形;硬往里塞 skill 几乎没什么收益。"组合模式"是一个工具,不是教条——它能真正产出价值时再用,只会让本不需要复杂化的流程变复杂时,就别碰。
A second practical caveat concerns description engineering. For the composition to fire correctly, the skill descriptions and the subagent descriptions both need to be written well. The subagent description determines whether the subagent is dispatched. The skill description determines whether the skill activates inside the dispatched subagent. Both layers of triggering need to work for the composition to produce its intended behavior. The same disciplines from Chapter Six apply here: canonical statement, trigger language, boundary, input expectation. The developer who has internalized those disciplines can debug failed compositions quickly. The developer who has not will sometimes find that the composition does not work and will not be sure why.
第二条务实提醒,关于 description 的工程化。要让组合正确触发,skill 的 description 和 subagent 的 description 都得写好。Subagent 的 description 决定它会不会被派出去;skill 的 description 决定它会不会在那个被派出去的 subagent 内部被激活。两层触发都得正常,组合才会产出预期的行为。第六章里那套规矩在这里同样适用:规范化的陈述、触发语、边界、输入预期。已经把那套规矩内化了的开发者,排错时能很快找到失败的组合;没有内化的开发者,有时会面对"组合不工作、却说不清为什么"的窘境。
A third worked example will deepen the pattern. The developer has installed the documentation-writer subagent and a project-specific skill called doc-style-guide, which encodes the team's conventions for technical writing: the preferred section structure, the rules about voice and tense, the format of code examples, and the policy on what level of detail is appropriate for different audiences. The skill is project-specific because every team has its own documentation style, and a documentation-writer subagent that wrote in some generic style would produce documentation that did not fit the team's existing corpus.
第三个完整例子,把模式讲得更透。开发者已装好 documentation-writer subagent,以及一个项目级的 skill,叫 doc-style-guide——它把团队在技术写作上的全部规约都编码在内:偏好的章节结构、关于"语气/时态"的规则、代码示例的格式、对不同受众该铺多少细节的策略。这个 skill 是项目级的,因为每个团队的文档风格都不一样;一个 documentation-writer subagent 如果产出的是泛泛的"通用风格",那么写出来的文档将和团队既有的整套文档语料不搭。
← Chapter 14 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 132 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) 132
The composition produces a documentation-writer that is doubly informed. The subagent provides the role: a senior technical writer who knows how to explain code clearly, how to choose examples, and how to keep documentation honest. The skill provides the project conventions: section ordering, voice, tense, example format. When the developer dispatches the subagent on a new module that needs documenting, both fire together. The output reads like good technical writing in the team's house style, because both layers of expertise are present at once. The developer did not have to brief the subagent on the team's conventions, because the skill brought them automatically. She did not have to teach the skill how to write good prose, because the subagent provided that. Each system did its job, and the composition produced output better than either alone.
这一组合,产出的是"双重知情"的 documentation-writer。Subagent 给出"角色":一位资深技术作者,懂得如何把代码讲清楚、如何挑选例子、如何保证文档的诚实。Skill 给出"项目规约":章节顺序、语气、时态、例子格式。当开发者把 subagent 派去写一个待文档化的新模块时,两层同时点火。产出的文字读起来就像团队自家风格里的"好的技术写作"——因为两层专长同时在场。开发者不必再向 subagent 简报团队的规约,skill 自动带来了;也不必教 skill 怎么写出好散文,subagent 那头就备好了。各自做各自该做的事,而组合的产出,胜过任一者孤军作战的结果。

The Architectural Principle · 架构原则

There is a deeper architectural principle visible here, which is worth naming explicitly because it will guide every later chapter of the book. The composition pattern is an instance of the broader principle of separation of concerns. Skills hold project knowledge. Subagents hold roles and isolate contexts. Neither system tries to do the other's job. When a workflow needs both kinds of capability, the systems compose, and the composition produces capabilities that neither alone could provide. This is the same principle that underlies every well-architected piece of software, applied to the design of a Claude Code environment. Developers familiar with separation of concerns in their own code will recognize the principle immediately and find the composition pattern intuitive. Developers unfamiliar with it will, by working through this part of the book, internalize the principle by seeing it applied to a domain where its benefits are immediate and visible.
这里其实浮现出一条更深的架构原则,值得显式点出来——因为它会指导本书之后每一章。"组合模式"其实是更广义的"关注点分离"原则的一个实例。Skill 持有"项目知识";subagent 持有"角色"并隔离上下文。任一系统都不试图去做对方的工作。当一个工作流同时需要这两种能力时,系统就组合起来,而组合的产出,又超出它们各自能独立提供的上限。这与"每一段良好架构的软件"所遵循的是同一条原则,只是被搬到了 Claude Code 环境的设计上来。熟悉"关注点分离"这条原则的开发者,会立刻认出它,并觉得组合模式很自然;不熟悉的开发者,只要走完本书这一部分,也就会在"一个收益立竿见影、看得见摸得着"的领域里把它内化下来。
A practical implication of this principle is the design discipline for adding new capabilities to a Claude Code environment. When the developer notices a recurring kind of work that her current configuration handles poorly, she should ask, is the gap a knowledge gap or a role gap. If she finds herself wishing the environment knew something specific, that is a knowledge gap, and the answer is a skill. If she finds herself wishing a different kind of attention were being applied to a kind of work, that is a role gap, and the answer is a subagent. Sometimes the gap is both, and the
这条原则的一条实务含义,是给 Claude Code 环境"加新能力"时的设计纪律。当开发者注意到有一类反复出现的工作、当前的配置处理得不好时,她应当先问一句:这道缺口,究竟是"知识缺口"还是"角色缺口"?如果她希望"环境知道某件具体的事"——那是知识缺口,答案是写一个 skill。如果她希望"在处理某类工作时,投入的是另一种不同的注意力"——那是角色缺口,答案是写一个 subagent。有时候这道缺口两边都站,那
← Chapter 14 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 133 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) 133
answer is a composition. Asking the right question first prevents the developer from building skills that should have been subagents, or subagents that should have been skills, both of which are common mistakes that produce environments that work but feel awkward. Asking the right question consistently produces environments that fit the developer's work cleanly, because each capability lives in the right place.
答案是"组合"。先问对问题,就能避免那种"本该写 skill 却被写成了 subagent"或"本该写 subagent 却被写成了 skill"的常见错误——这两种错,都能让环境跑得起来,但用起来总是别扭。每次都问对,就能让每一项能力都"住"在它最该住的地方,环境就能干净地贴合开发者的实际工作。
A second design discipline that emerges from the composition pattern concerns versioning. Skills and subagents that are designed to compose with each other should evolve together. If the developer changes the conventions encoded in a skill, the subagents that depend on that skill may need to be checked to ensure their behavior still aligns. If the developer reshapes a subagent's role, the skills designed to fit that role may need to be revisited. This coupling is loose, not tight, and the systems remain independently functional, but the composition is most reliable when the developer treats related skills and subagents as parts of a single design rather than as unrelated artifacts. The discipline is to version-control the entire .claude directory, to commit related changes together, and to review skill and subagent changes side by side when they are likely to affect each other.
从组合模式里浮现出来的第二条设计纪律,关于版本管理。设计上要彼此组合的 skill 与 subagent,应当"共同演进"。如果开发者改了某条 skill 里编码的规约,那些依赖它的 subagent 可能要回头检查一下,看自身行为是否还对得上;如果她重塑了某个 subagent 的角色,那些为该角色"贴身打造"的 skill 也可能需要再审视。这种耦合是"松"而不是"紧"的,系统各自依旧独立可用;但是,当开发者把"相互关联的 skill 与 subagent"当作同一个设计里的若干部件、而不是彼此不相关的零散制品时,组合才最可靠。具体纪律是:把整个 .claude 目录纳入版本控制、把相关变更一起提交,在它们可能彼此影响时,把 skill 与 subagent 的变更并排做评审。
One more reflection on the experience of the composition pattern, before this chapter closes. The first time a developer sees a skill activate inside a subagent and watches the combined behavior produce something neither could have produced alone, the experience tends to be memorable. There is a moment of recognition in which the architecture stops feeling abstract and starts feeling like a real engineering substrate. The skill library and the subagent library, which until then had felt like two separate collections of useful artifacts, become something larger. They become the components of an environment, and the environment becomes the place where the developer's real work happens. This shift in perception is not just aesthetic. It is the shift from using Claude Code to engineering with Claude Code, which is the central promise of this book and the moment the entire framework starts paying back the investment of learning it.
在收束本章之前,再就"组合模式的体验"做一点感想。开发者第一次亲眼看见"一个 skill 在某个 subagent 内部被激活",并看着两者合起来的行为产出了"任何一方单独都产不出"的东西时,这份体验往往是令人难忘的。会有一刻"认出"——架构不再显得抽象,而是开始像一块真正可工程化的底材。原本感觉像"两套互不相干的实用制品"的 skill 库与 subagent 库,忽然变成了某种更大的东西:它们成为一块"环境"的组件;而这块环境,反过来成为开发者真正的工作"发生的地方"。这种认知上的迁移,不只是审美层面的——它正是"从用 Claude Code"到"用 Claude Code 做工程化"的跃迁。这正是本书的核心承诺,也是整副框架开始回报"为学会它所付出的投入"的那一刻。
A reflection on the architecture, before this chapter and this part of the book end. The design choices that made skills and subagents into separate systems, rather than merging them into a single mechanism, were deliberate. Skills are extensible knowledge. Subagents are specialized roles
在收束本章、也收束本书这一部分之前,再来对架构本身做一点反思。把 skill 与 subagent 设计为两套独立系统、而不是合并成一套机制,是经过深思熟虑的。Skill 是"可扩展的知识";subagent 是"专门化的角色",
← Chapter 14 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 134 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) 134
with isolated contexts. The two are different kinds of thing, and treating them as different kinds of thing keeps the design clean. A merged system would have been simpler at first glance and worse in practice. The cost of having two systems is the small overhead of learning both. The benefit of having two systems is that each remains focused on what it does best, and the composition between them is principled rather than tangled. The reader who has worked through the last six chapters now has both systems in working memory and in installed form, and she has seen how they reinforce each other to produce capabilities neither could produce alone.
并配以隔离的上下文。两者本质上是不同种类的"东西",把它们当作不同种类的"东西"来对待,设计才能保持清爽。合并的系统初看更简洁,实操起来却更糟。维持两套系统的代价,是同时学两套系统那点小小开销;收益则是,每套系统都聚焦在自己最擅长的事上,二者之间的组合是有章法的、而非缠成一团。走过过去六章的读者,如今两套系统都在手上、也都在已安装的形态里,也已经见过它们如何彼此增益,产出单靠任一者都达不到的能力。

Second-Order Benefits · 二阶收益

There is a second-order benefit to the composition pattern that becomes visible only after a developer has lived with a composed library for a few months. The benefit is that the library becomes self-documenting in a way that monolithic configurations are not. A new developer joining the project can read the .claude/skills directory to learn the project's conventions; reading the SKILL.md files is essentially reading a structured guide to how the team prefers to work. The same new developer can read the .claude/agents directory to learn what specialist roles the team treats as standing capabilities; reading the subagent definitions is essentially reading the team's job descriptions for its AI-assisted workflow. Together, the two directories form a piece of working documentation that is more accurate than any wiki, because the documentation is the configuration the tool actually uses. This self-documenting property is a substantial benefit for any team that grows, because it lowers the cost of onboarding new members and reduces the gap between what the team says it does and what the team actually does.
组合模式还有一条"二阶收益",只在开发者与组合库相伴共处几个月之后才看得清:它让这套库具备一种"自我文档化"的性质,而单体化的配置做不到这一点。新加入项目的开发者,读一读 .claude/skills 目录就能学懂项目规约——读那些 SKILL.md 文件,本质上就是在读一份"团队偏好如何工作"的结构化指南。同一位新人,再读读 .claude/agents 目录,就能了解团队把哪些"专家角色"当作常设能力——读那些 subagent 定义,本质上就是在读"团队 AI 辅助工作流"里的岗位说明书。两个目录合起来,就构成了一份"比任何 wiki 都更准确"的工作文档——因为文档本身,就是工具真正在用的那份配置。对任何会成长的团队,这种自我文档化的属性都是一笔不小的收益:它降低了新成员上手的成本,也缩短了"团队声称在做的事"与"团队实际在做的事"之间的鸿沟。
A third reflection concerns the relationship between this composition and the third pillar of the framework, hooks, which is the subject of the part of the book that follows. The composition of skills and subagents that this chapter has taught is internal to the agent loop. Both skills and subagents operate within the probabilistic space of the language model. They shape what the model does, but they do not impose deterministic constraints on it. Hooks are different. Hooks live outside the loop and impose deterministic constraints on it. The fully engineered Claude Code environment that the book is building toward includes all three pillars working together: skills
第三点反思,关于"本章所讲的组合"与"框架第三根支柱"——hooks——之间的关系,hooks 是本书下一部分的主题。本章所教的"skill + subagent 组合",发生于 agent loop 内部;skill 与 subagent 都在语言模型的概率性空间里运作,它们塑造模型的行为,但并不对它施加硬性约束。Hooks 则是另一回事——它位于 loop 之外,会对 loop 施加确定性约束。本书一路要带读者搭建出来的那块"完整工程化的 Claude Code 环境",靠的是三根支柱协同工作:skill
← Chapter 14 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 135 页 · CHAPTER FOURTEEN(续) · PART THREE 收束 135
providing capability, subagents providing specialization, hooks providing the deterministic edges that make the whole environment safe and reliable to depend on. The reader who has finished this chapter has half of the architecture in place. The next part of the book completes it.
提供"能力",subagent 提供"专门化",hooks 则提供"确定性边缘"——正是它让整块环境可以放心依赖、安全可信。读到这里,读者手里的架构已经搭好了一半;下一部分书,把它补完。
A short observation is worth recording about the experience of working in a fully composed environment, although the reader will only verify the observation for herself once she has built one. Developers report that the felt difference between Claude Code without a configured environment and Claude Code with a thoughtful skills-plus-subagents composition is larger than they expected. The unconfigured tool feels capable but generic. The configured tool feels specific to the developer's work in a way that is initially surprising. Tasks that used to require explicit briefing now run themselves. Reviews that used to take ten minutes finish in three. The output that used to need editing for tone and convention arrives already in the right voice. The whole working day reshapes itself around the new floor of capability, and the developer's attention rises to fill the new space the environment has created. This is the practical payoff of the composition pattern, and it is the payoff that justifies the investment of time in building skill and subagent libraries thoughtfully rather than casually.
关于"在一块完全组合好的环境里工作"的体验,有一点观察值得先记下来——但读者要等自己真的搭出一套,才能亲自验证。开发者普遍反映:"未做配置的 Claude Code"和"做了深思熟虑的 skill+subagent 组合的 Claude Code"之间的体感差距,比他们预想的要大。未配置的工具"有能耐,但泛";配置过的工具"贴着开发者的具体工作而来",那份"贴",一开始甚至有点令人惊讶。以前需要专门简报才能跑起来的任务,如今自己就跑完了;以前要花十分钟的评审,现在三分钟就收;以前总要回头改语气和规约的产出,如今一上来就是对的嗓音。整个工作日,会围绕这块"新的能力底线"重新排布,开发者的注意力随之向上抬,去填满环境新腾出来的空间。这就是组合模式在实务层面的回报——也是"花时间认真搭一套 skill 库与 subagent 库"这件事之所以值得的根本原因。
Part Three ends here. The reader has, over five chapters, learned the architecture of subagents, mastered the design of single specialists, internalized the orchestrator pattern that turns sequential work into parallel work, installed a library of eight working specialists, and seen how skills and subagents compose to produce the deeper architectural power of the system. The second pillar of the framework is now in place. The third pillar, hooks, is the subject of the next part of the book. Hooks are how the probabilistic system gets wrapped in deterministic edges, how the developer ensures that the things that must happen always happen, and how the boundaries of the engineered environment become unbreakable at the points where breakage would matter most. That work begins in the chapter ahead.
第三部分至此收束。读者用五章的时间,学会了 subagent 的架构、掌握了单个专家的设计、把"把串行变并行"的编排器模式内化下来,装好了一个含八位"能干活"的专家的库,也亲眼见到了 skill 与 subagent 是如何组合成系统更深层架构力量的。框架的第二根支柱,至此就位。第三根支柱——hooks——是本书下一部分的主题:它正是"用确定性的边缘去包裹一个概率性的系统"的那条路径,正是"必须发生的事永远会发生"那条工程化保障,也正是"工程化环境的边界,在最不该破的地方变得不可破"那条具体落地方式。这件事,由下一章开始做。
Before turning the page, a short closing thought. Each part of this book has built one layer of the larger architecture. Part One established the mental model. Part Two installed the first pillar. Part Three has installed the second pillar and shown how the first two pillars compose. The pattern of work that the book asks of the reader is, in this respect, accumulating. Each part builds on what came before, and each successive layer makes the
翻页之前,还有一句简短的收束语。本书的每一部分,都搭建了大架构中的一层:第一部分搭起心智模型;第二部分装上第一根支柱;第三部分装上第二根支柱,并展示了前两根如何组合。从这个意义上,本书要求读者投入的工作,是"累积"的——每一部分都建在前一部分之上,而后续每一层,都会让前一层
← Chapter 14 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 136 页 · PART THREE 收束 136
previous layers more powerful by giving them a richer environment to operate in. The hooks that the next part introduces will, in turn, make the skills and subagents already installed more reliable, because the deterministic guarantees that hooks provide will protect the probabilistic systems below them from the failure modes those probabilistic systems cannot prevent on their own. The architecture is genuinely hierarchical, and the experience of building the upper layers is, over time, the experience of giving the lower layers a sturdier foundation to stand on.
变得更强,因为它给了它们一个更富的环境去运行。下一部分要引入的 hook,反过来又会让已经装好的 skill 与 subagent 更加可靠——因为 hook 所提供的那些"确定性保证",会把下层那些"概率性系统"无法独自防住的失效模式,在它发生之前就拦下来。整套架构是真正分层级的;而搭上层这件事,日积月累,就是把下层的底座越筑越稳的那份体验。
