The discipline of writing hooks that are easy to maintain is, in the end, the discipline that determines whether the bundle remains useful over the years or quietly drifts into uselessness.

The Team Dimension · 团队维度

A fifth observation concerns the team dimension of the security bundle. Security hooks are valuable when one developer installs them, and they are dramatically more valuable when an entire team installs the same set across all of its projects. A team that has agreed on a standard security bundle, encoded in shared configuration that travels with each project, raises the floor of safety for everyone on the team simultaneously. New team members inherit the bundle automatically the moment they clone any project. The team's collective risk of an incident drops sharply, because the most common categories of accident are blocked across every developer's environment, not just in the environments of the developers who happened to install hooks personally. This is the team-level argument for hooks, and it is the argument that justifies treating the security bundle as a shared engineering asset rather than as a personal preference. Teams that adopt this view tend to have strikingly fewer incidents than teams that leave each developer to her own preferences, and the difference is not subtle.
第五条观察,关于安全套件的"团队维度"。单个开发者装上安全 hook,是有价值的;而当整个团队在所有项目上都装上同一套,价值会被大幅放大。一个团队若就"标准安全套件"达成共识,并把它编码进随每个项目走的共享配置里,就能同时抬高全员的安全底线。新成员一克隆任何项目,套件就自动继承到手;团队的事故总风险会急剧下降——因为最常见的那几类意外,在每一位开发者的环境里都已被拦下,而不是只落在"碰巧个人装了 hook"的那几位开发者身上。这是"hook 必要性"的团队级论据,也正是它把"安全套件"从"个人偏好"提升为"团队工程资产"的那条理由。秉持这种看法的团队,通常比"把偏好留给每个人自己"的团队,事故少得多——这份差异,可不是"细微"。
A sixth and final observation concerns the relationship between security hooks and the other two pillars of the framework. Skills and subagents both shape what Claude does within the agent loop, and both can encode warnings about dangerous operations. A skill could include a directive to never run rm -rf, and a subagent system prompt could include a similar warning. These shapings are useful, but they are not enough on their own,
第六条、也是最后一条观察,关于"安全 hook"与框架另外两根支柱之间的关系。Skill 与 subagent,都塑造 Claude 在 agent loop 内部的行为,也都可以把"危险操作的警告"编码在内。一条 skill 可以包含"绝对不要跑 rm -rf"这种指令,一个 subagent 的 system prompt 也可以放一段类似的警告。这些塑造是有用的,但单靠它们并不够——
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 166 页 · CHAPTER SEVENTEEN 166
because they ride on the model's probabilistic compliance with its instructions. The security hook is what turns the warning into a guarantee. The right architectural pattern is to have all three: a skill that explains what good practice looks like, a subagent that operates within that practice, and a hook that enforces the boundary that even the best skill and subagent cannot guarantee. The three layers reinforce each other. The skill teaches. The subagent applies. The hook enforces. The combination is stronger than any one layer alone, and developers who think of security as a single-layer concern miss the architectural elegance that the three-pillar approach provides.
因为它们都"骑"在模型对其指令的概率性遵循之上。安全 hook,才是把"警告"变成"保证"的那一层。正确的架构模式,是三层都要有:一条 skill 把"好的实践是什么样的"讲清楚;一个 subagent 在那条实践里做事;一条 hook 把边界强制住——这条边界,即便再好的 skill 和 subagent 也无法保证自己不去踩。三层彼此增益:skill 教;subagent 照做;hook 强制。组合起来,比任一层单兵作战都要强;把"安全"当成"单层问题"看的开发者,会错过三支柱方法在架构上的那份优雅。
A third reflection concerns the relationship between hooks and trust. Without hooks, the developer has to trust the model on every action. Trust, in this context, means hoping that the model will interpret her requests correctly and will not produce dangerous side effects. Trust is appropriate for many things, but it is not appropriate for catastrophic failure modes, because the failure rate of any probabilistic system is non-zero, and a non-zero failure rate over many actions eventually produces a failure. Hooks change the relationship. With hooks, the developer trusts the model for most things and relies on mechanism for the things that matter. This is a better division of labor than asking the model to be perfect, because perfection is not what models are good at, and mechanism is exactly what mechanism is good at.
第三条反思,关于 hook 与"信任"的关系。没有 hook,开发者就只能在每一次动作上信任模型。这里的"信任",其实质是"指望着模型正确解读她的请求,且不产生危险的副作用"。对很多事,这种信任是恰当的;但对"灾难性失效"那一类,这种信任并不恰当——任何概率性系统,其失败率都不为 0;而"不为 0 的失败率"摊在足够多的动作上,迟早会引发一次失败。Hook 改变了这种关系:有了 hook,开发者把多数事交给模型去信任,把"真正要紧的事"交给机制去兜底。这比"要求模型完美"是更好的分工——因为"完美"本就不是模型所擅长的;而"机制",恰恰是机制所擅长的。
A reflection before this chapter closes. Security hooks have a quiet quality that distinguishes them from skills and subagents. Skills produce visible value: every commit message, every PR description, every refactor proposal is something the developer sees and uses. Subagents produce visible value too: every parallel review, every specialized output is a tangible artifact. Security hooks produce value that is mostly invisible. Most days, no security hook fires, because the developer is not asking Claude to do anything dangerous. The hooks sit silently. Their value reveals itself only on the day when something goes wrong and a hook stops it from going more wrong. That day, the value of the entire bundle is realized in a single block. The asymmetry, low ongoing cost and high single-event payoff, is what makes security hooks worth installing even if they never fire. The developer who waits until something goes wrong to install them has discovered the value of hooks at the worst possible moment. The
本章收束前的反思:安全 hook 有一种"安静"的特质,把它与 skill、subagent 区分开。Skill 产出的是可见的价值——每一条 commit message、每一条 PR 描述、每一份重构建议,都是开发者看得见、用得上的东西。Subagent 也产出可见的价值——每一次并行评审、每一份专家产物,都是有形有质的工件。安全 hook 产出的价值,几乎全在暗处。大多数日子里,没有安全 hook 跳出来——因为开发者根本没让 Claude 去干危险的事。Hook 们安安静静地蹲着;它们的价值,只在某天"出了岔子、而某条 hook 把更大的岔子拦下"那一刻,才浮出水面。那一天,整套装件的价值,以一次拦截兑现。这种"日常成本极低、单次事件回报极高"的不对称,正是安全 hook 哪怕一次都没真正触发,也仍然值得装上的原因。等到"出了事再装"的开发者,是在最不该的时机才看清 hook 的价值。
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 167 页 · CHAPTER SEVENTEEN(收束) 167
developer who installs them as part of her standard environment has bought insurance she will probably never use, which is exactly the kind of insurance worth having.
把安全 hook 作为标准环境的一部分装上的开发者,买了一份自己大概永远用不到的"保险"——而这恰恰是"值得买的保险"的本意。
The next chapter takes hooks one more step into composition. With single hooks installed, the question becomes how multiple hooks work together, how event pipelines emerge when hooks chain, and how the developer can construct sophisticated lifecycle workflows out of the same simple primitives. Hook chains are the final piece of hook design, and they are the subject of the chapter ahead.
下一章,把 hook 再推一步,推入"组合"。单条 hook 装好之后,真正的问题变成:多条 hook 如何协作?hook 链化时,事件流水线如何浮现?开发者又如何用同一批简单的原语,搭出精细的生命周期工作流?Hook 链是 hook 设计最后一块拼图,也是下一章要讲的事。

原书第 168 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 168

CHAPTER EIGHTEEN · 第十八章
Hook Chains and Event Pipelines · Hook 链与事件流水线

"A pipeline is what happens when many small actions, each useful in itself, are arranged so that the output of one becomes the context of the next." — Doug McIlroy, paraphrased
"所谓流水线,就是把许多本身各自有用的小动作,安排成'上一个的输出,成为下一个的上下文'。" —— 道格·麦克罗伊(意译)
This chapter is the final treatment of hooks before the book turns to composition across all three pillars. The previous three chapters built hooks one at a time: the mechanics, the design patterns, the security applications. This chapter puts hooks together. When multiple hooks fire on the same event, when hooks pass information forward through the lifecycle, when the developer wants a coordinated workflow that involves several lifecycle moments rather than just one, the resulting structure is a hook chain or an event pipeline. This chapter teaches both.
本章是关于 hook 的最后一章,之后书就要谈"三根支柱如何彼此组合"了。前三章,是一根一根地讲 hook:机制、设计模式、安全应用。本章要把 hook 装到一起。多条 hook 在同一事件上触发、hook 在生命周期里把信息往前传、开发者想要的不止一个生命周期节点而是几个——这些情况所衍生出来的结构,就是 hook 链或事件流水线。本章把两者都讲一遍。
Begin with the simpler case. A hook chain is what happens when more than one hook is configured under the same event with the same matcher. Claude Code runs them in the order they are listed in the configuration file. Each hook receives the same input on standard input. Each hook's exit code is independent: one hook's decision to allow or block does not affect what the next hook does. The chain proceeds through all hooks unless one of them blocks, in which case the action is blocked and subsequent hooks in the same chain do not run. The output of each hook is collected, but the hooks themselves do not communicate directly with each other. Communication, when it is wanted, happens through side effects on the file system or through environment variables.
从简单的那种情形讲起。Hook 链,就是"同一个事件、同一个 matcher 下配了不止一条 hook"时发生的事。Claude Code 按配置文件中列出的顺序执行它们。每条 hook 都在标准输入上收到同样的输入;每条 hook 的退出码彼此独立——一条 hook 的"放行/拦截"决定,不影响下一条 hook。链会一路走完整段,除非其中某条拦下,这时动作被拦、链上后续的 hook 不再跑。每条 hook 的输出会被收集,但 hook 彼此之间并不直接通信。需要通信的时候,就通过文件系统的副作用,或通过环境变量。
The Prettier-and-ESLint example from Chapter Sixteen is a small chain. Two hooks fire on the same PostToolUse event with the same matcher. The first runs Prettier; the second runs ESLint. The order matters in this case, because Prettier reformats the file and ESLint then applies its fixes on the reformatted version. If the order were reversed, the result would be subtly different in some edge cases. The developer who designs a chain should think about whether the order of hooks is meaningful, and if it is, she should comment the configuration to make the order explicit for future readers.
第十六章的 Prettier + ESLint 例子,就是一条小链:两条 hook 在同一个 PostToolUse 事件、同一个 matcher 下触发。第一条跑 Prettier,第二条跑 ESLint。这里顺序重要——Prettier 把文件重新格式化,ESLint 再在格式化后的版本上施加它的修复;若顺序反过来,某些边界情形会得到微妙不同的结果。设计 hook 链的开发者,应当想清楚"顺序是否有意义";若有,就该在配置里加注释,让顺序对未来的读者也是显式的。
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 169 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 169
A second example, slightly more elaborate, shows a chain that combines a notifier with a guard. The configuration runs after every Bash command that Claude executes: it logs the command to an audit file, then checks whether the command has produced any new git changes, and if so, writes a small note to a session-summary file that the developer can review at the end of the day.
第二个例子稍复杂一点,展示一条把 notifier 与 guard 组合起来的链。配置在 Claude 跑完每一条 Bash 命令后触发:先把命令写进审计文件,再检查该命令是否产生了新的 git 变更;若有,就在一份"当日 session 摘要"文件里写一条小备注,供开发者晚些时候回看。
{
  "hooks": {
    "PostToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Bash",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "jq -r '\"\\(.session_id)\\t\\(.tool_input.command)\"' >> ~/.claude/bash-history.log"
          },
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "if git status --porcelain 2>/dev/null | grep -q . ; then jq -r '.tool_input.command' >> ~/.claude/session-summary.txt; fi"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
(配置含义:在 PostToolUse 上挂两条 hook 形成的链,matcher 限定 Bash 工具;第一条以制表符分隔的 " \t " 格式追加到 ~/.claude/bash-history.log;第二条检查 git status --porcelain 若有变更,就把该命令追加到 ~/.claude/session-summary.txt。)

Two Hooks in Sequence · 两条 hook 串行

The two hooks fire in sequence on the same event. The first appends a tab-separated record to the audit log. The second checks the git status and, if changes are present, appends the command to a session summary. Both run on every Bash call. Both are silent in the success case. Together, they produce an audit trail and a daily summary without any conscious intervention from the developer.
这两条 hook 在同一事件上串行触发。第一条向审计日志追加一条 tab 分隔的记录;第二条检查 git 状态,若有变更就把命令追加到 session 摘要。两者在每次 Bash 调用时都跑;两者在成功路径上都沉默。合起来,就能在开发者完全无须主动介入的情况下,产出一份审计轨迹和一份当日摘要。
Move to event pipelines, which is the more sophisticated case. An event pipeline is a workflow that spans multiple lifecycle events, with hooks at each event reinforcing or extending the others. The classic example is a git pipeline: a SessionStart hook ensures the working tree is clean and reports the
进入事件流水线——这是更复杂的那一种情形。事件流水线,是一条跨越多个生命周期事件的工作流;每个事件上都挂着 hook,彼此强化、彼此延展。经典例子就是一条 git 流水线:SessionStart hook 确保工作树是干净的,并汇报
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 170 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 170
current branch, a PreToolUse hook on Bash blocks force-pushes to main, a PostToolUse hook on Edit or Write runs the formatter, a Stop hook checks whether tests pass before allowing the session to end with uncommitted changes. None of these hooks knows about the others directly. But together they form a pipeline that wraps every git-related action in a consistent set of guarantees, and the developer who has installed the pipeline experiences her git work differently because every step is now defended in the right place.
当前分支;Bash 上的 PreToolUse hook 拦截 force-push 到 main;Edit/Write 上的 PostToolUse hook 跑格式化;Stop hook 在 session 结束前检查测试是否通过,以免"session 结束时还存在未提交的变更"。这些 hook 彼此并不直接"知道"对方的存在;但合在一起,它们构成了一条流水线,把每一项与 git 相关的动作,都包进同一套一致的保证里。装上这条流水线的开发者,她的 git 工作会"感觉不一样"——每一步都恰好守在它该被守的地方。
A worked event pipeline is presented below. The configuration is more substantial than any in the previous chapters, because it is doing real cross-event coordination. The pipeline assumes a project where tests are run with npm test, the main branch is named main, and the developer wants Claude Code to be especially careful with anything that touches that branch.
下面是一条完整的事件流水线。配置比前几章任何一条都更厚实——它在跨事件做实在的协调。这条流水线假定:测试用 npm test 跑、主分支名为 main、开发者希望 Claude Code 对"触及主分支"这件事格外小心。
{
  "hooks": {
    "SessionStart": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "echo '## Branch'; git branch --show-current; echo '## Status'; git status --short"
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "PreToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Bash",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/block-dangerous.sh"
          },
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/block-force-push.sh"
          }
        ]
      },
      {
        "matcher": "Edit|Write|MultiEdit",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/protect-files.sh"
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "PostToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Edit|Write|MultiEdit",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "FILE=$(jq -r '.tool_input.file_path'); npx prettier --write \"$FILE\" 2>/dev/null || true"
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "Stop": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "if [ \"$(git status --porcelain | wc -l)\" -gt 0 ]; then echo '{\"decision\":\"block\",\"reason\":\"Uncommitted changes present. Commit or stash before ending the session.\"}'; fi"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
(配置含义:这是一个跨四个生命周期事件的完整 git 流水线。SessionStart:打印当前分支和 git status --short,注入到 Claude 的启动上下文;PreToolUse(Bash):同时挂 block-dangerous.sh 和 block-force-push.sh 两条 gate;PreToolUse(Edit|Write|MultiEdit):挂 protect-files.sh 一条 gate;PostToolUse(Edit|Write|MultiEdit):跑 Prettier 格式化;Stop:若有未提交变更,向 stdout 打 JSON 决策块 {"decision":"block","reason":"..."},让模型无法在带未提交变更的情况下结束 session。)
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 171 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 171
Read the configuration carefully, because it covers four lifecycle events and demonstrates how a serious pipeline holds together. SessionStart runs once when the session begins and prints the current branch and any uncommitted changes; the output is injected into Claude's starting context, so Claude knows the state of the world at the start of every session. PreToolUse on Bash runs the dangerous-command and force-push gates from Chapter Seventeen. PreToolUse on Edit and Write runs the protected-files gate. PostToolUse on Edit and Write runs Prettier. Stop runs a check that uses the JSON output format to refuse the session's end if there are uncommitted
请把这份配置仔细读一遍——它覆盖了四个生命周期事件,示范了一条严肃的流水线是怎么"粘合"起来的。SessionStart 在 session 启动时跑一次,打印当前分支以及任何未提交的变更;输出会被注入到 Claude 的启动上下文里,因此 Claude 在每个 session 一开始就"知道外面世界长什么样"。PreToolUse 在 Bash 上挂第十七章讲过的"危险命令"与"force-push"两条 gate。PreToolUse 在 Edit / Write 上挂"受保护文件"的 gate。PostToolUse 在 Edit / Write 上跑 Prettier。Stop 做一项"用 JSON 输出格式"做判定:若存在未提交
changes; the JSON output format is what allows the Stop event to actually refuse the end of the session, because the standard exit-code mechanism cannot stop a session that is already ending. The pipeline as a whole ensures that: Claude always knows the branch and clean-status state of the world; dangerous shell commands are blocked; force pushes to main are blocked; protected files cannot be edited; every file is formatted; and the session cannot end with uncommitted changes. The combination is a substantial improvement over the unconfigured environment, and it costs about a hundred lines of JSON to install.
变更,就拒绝 session 结束。这里之所以要"用 JSON 输出格式"——是因为 Stop 事件对应的"会话即将结束"这件事,光靠"退出码"那条机制拦不住,只有 JSON 决策块才拦得住。这条流水线作为整体,保证了:Claude 总能知道当前分支和 clean-status 状态;危险的 shell 命令被拦;force-push 到 main 被拦;受保护文件改不了;每一个文件都被格式化;session 不会在"带未提交变更"的状态下结束。这套组合,比未配置的环境是一个显著升级;而装好它总共才百来行 JSON。

The Power of Coordination · 协调的力量

The key insight from this pipeline is the coordination across events. None of the individual hooks is doing something the developer could not do manually. She could, in principle, run Prettier by hand after every Claude edit, run git status by hand before every commit, refuse to allow force pushes by typing the wrong command into her own terminal. The point of the pipeline is not that any individual hook is doing something new. The point is that the developer no longer has to remember to do any of it. The pipeline remembers for her. The cumulative effect of having all these little guarantees enforced mechanically, rather than remembered, is that the developer's attention is freed for the parts of the work that genuinely require attention. The mechanical parts run mechanically. The interesting parts get the focus they deserve.
这条流水线带出的关键洞见,是"跨事件协调"本身。任一单条 hook 都没有做开发者"手动做不了"的事——她原则上可以每次 Claude 改完手动跑一次 Prettier、提交前手动跑一次 git status、想 force-push 时自己在终端里敲错命令拒绝自己。流水线真正的意义,并不在"哪一条 hook 做了什么新鲜事";而在于"开发者再也不必记得去做这些"。流水线替她记着。所有的这些"小事"由机制强制而不是靠记忆——其累积效果是:开发者的注意力,被释放出来,留给那些真正需要她注意的工作。机械的部分,机械地跑;有意思的部分,得到它应得的专注。
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 172 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 172
changes, returning a decision of block with a clear reason that surfaces to Claude.
变更,就以 block 决策回送,并附上一段清晰的 reason,送达 Claude。

A Coherent Workflow · 一条内洽的工作流

The pipeline together enforces a coherent workflow. Sessions begin with awareness of state. Tool calls are filtered through gates appropriate to their kind. Edits are followed by formatting. Sessions cannot end with the working tree dirty. The developer has not had to remember any of these rules, because each rule is enforced by a hook at the right lifecycle moment. The development experience is, in a sense, supervised, but the supervision is mechanical and silent, and it intervenes only when the developer was about to do something the pipeline considers a mistake.
这条流水线,作为一个整体,强制的是一条内洽的工作流:session 启动时即知"世界状态";工具调用按各自类型被相应的 gate 过滤;编辑后即格式化;session 不会在 dirty 工作树的状态下收尾。开发者不再需要记得这些规则中的任何一条——每条规则都被一个 hook 守在它"该被守的"那个生命周期节点上。从某种意义上,这种开发体验是被"监督"着的;但这种监督是机械的、沉默的,只在开发者正要做"流水线认为是个错"的某件事时,才插一手。
A discipline applies to event pipelines that is worth stating directly. Pipelines are powerful, but they are also a form of accumulated complexity, and every hook in a pipeline is a hook the developer is committing to maintain. A small pipeline of three or four well-chosen hooks is a substantial improvement over no pipeline at all. A pipeline of fifteen hooks may be worse than seven, because the failure modes multiply, the cumulative latency adds up, and the developer's ability to debug an unexpected behavior degrades when many hooks are running. The recommendation is to start small, add hooks one at a time, and remove any hook that has not earned its place after a month of use. The pipeline should serve the developer, not the other way around, and a pipeline that has grown into a maintenance burden is a pipeline that has been over-engineered.
关于事件流水线,有一条值得直接点出的纪律:流水线很强,但它同时也是"累积复杂度"的一种形态;流水线里每条 hook,都是开发者承诺要维护的。一条三四条 hook 精心挑选的小流水线,比"完全没有流水线"已是显著升级;而十五条 hook 的大流水线,可能反而不如七条——因为失效模式成倍增长,累计延迟叠加,多 hook 共同运行时开发者 debug 异常行为的能力也会退化。推荐做法是:小起步、一次加一条、用了一个月还没证明自身价值的 hook 就拿掉。流水线应当服务于开发者,而不是反过来;一条"长成了维护负担"的流水线,就是被过度工程化的流水线。
A second discipline concerns testing. Every hook should be tested before it is committed. The standard testing pattern is to invoke the hook directly from the shell, passing in a representative JSON input on standard input, and observing the exit code and the standard error output. A hook that behaves correctly under direct testing usually behaves correctly inside Claude Code; a hook that does not has a chance of producing inscrutable failures inside the session. The fifteen minutes of direct testing at install time prevents debug sessions later. The pattern is worth adopting unconditionally for any hook that does anything more sophisticated than a one-liner.
第二条纪律关于测试。每条 hook 都应当在"提交"前测过。标准测试模式:在 shell 里直接调用它,在标准输入上喂一段"有代表性"的 JSON 输入,然后观察它的退出码和标准错误输出。在直接测试下行为正确的 hook,通常在 Claude Code 内也会正确;而直接测试下都不对的 hook,进了 session 大概率会吐出莫名其妙的失败。安装时花的这十五分钟直测,免去了日后的一整轮 debug。这套模式,任何"做的不止一行命令"的事,都值得无条件采用。
A second worked pipeline is worth presenting, because pipeline design is the kind of skill that matures only after a developer has seen several
第二条完整流水线值得摆出来——因为流水线设计这门手艺,只在看过若干条
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 173 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 173
substantial examples. The pipeline below is for a different shape of work: a content-generation project, where the developer is using Claude Code to produce written material rather than to write code. The pipeline reflects the different concerns of writing work. Drafts should be backed up automatically. Drafts should be passed through a humanizer pass after every edit. Sessions should end with a daily summary appended to a project journal. The pipeline below implements each of these.
比较"实在"的例子之后,才会真正成熟。下面这条流水线服务的,是一种不同形态的工作——一个"内容生成"项目,开发者用 Claude Code 产出的不是代码而是书面材料。流水线反映了写作这件事的关切:草稿应当自动备份;每次编辑之后,应当过一次"人味化"pass;session 结束时,应当向项目日志追加一份当日小结。下面这条流水线,把每一项都实现了。
{
  "hooks": {
    "SessionStart": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "echo '## Today, you are working on:'; cat $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/today.txt 2>/dev/null || echo 'No focus file. Set one in .claude/today.txt.'"
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "PostToolUse": [
      {
        "matcher": "Edit|Write",
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "FILE=$(jq -r '.tool_input.file_path'); cp \"$FILE\" \"$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.backups/$(basename $FILE).$(date +%s)\" 2>/dev/null || true",
            "async": true
          },
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "FILE=$(jq -r '.tool_input.file_path'); if echo \"$FILE\" | grep -qE '\\.(md|txt)$'; then $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/.claude/hooks/check-em-dashes.sh \"$FILE\"; fi"
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
    "SessionEnd": [
      {
        "hooks": [
          {
            "type": "command",
            "command": "echo \"$(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M') session ended. Files touched today:\" >> $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/journal.md; git diff --name-only HEAD~5..HEAD 2>/dev/null | head -10 >> $CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR/journal.md"
          }
        ]
      }
    ]
  }
}
(配置含义:这是一条"内容生成"项目的事件流水线。SessionStart:读 .claude/today.txt 焦点文件,把它注入到 Claude 的启动上下文;若文件不存在则给一条提示。PostToolUse(Edit|Write):挂两条 hook——第一条以 async: true 异步地把被编辑的文件备份到 .backups/<文件名>.<时间戳>,不影响 session 速度;第二条仅在文件后缀为 .md / .txt 时,调用 check-em-dashes.sh 检查"em-dash"等被禁用的写作习惯。SessionEnd:向项目 journal.md 追加一行"session 结束"加时间戳,再附上近 5 次提交里被改过的文件名清单。)
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 174 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 174
Read the pipeline carefully, because its shape is informative. SessionStart loads a focus file, which the developer has placed in the project to remind herself what she is working on today. The output is injected into Claude's starting context, so Claude knows the day's focus from the moment the session begins. PostToolUse fires two hooks on every Edit or Write. The first is asynchronous: it backs up the file to a timestamped copy in a backups directory, without slowing down the session. The second runs only on Markdown or text files, and it invokes a custom script that checks for em-dashes and other patterns the developer wants to avoid in her writing. SessionEnd appends a record to a project journal, giving the developer a continuous log of what she worked on across sessions. The pipeline does not block any of these actions. It does not interfere with Claude's flow. It quietly produces backups, applies the writing standard, and maintains the journal, all without conscious intervention.
请把这条流水线仔细读一遍——它的形状本身就是有信息量的。SessionStart 加载一份"焦点文件",这是开发者放在项目里、用来提醒自己"今天做什么"的;其输出会被注入到 Claude 的启动上下文,于是 Claude 在 session 一开始就掌握了"今日焦点"。PostToolUse 在每次 Edit / Write 上挂两条 hook——第一条是异步的:它把文件复制成一份带时间戳的备份,放进 backups 目录,并不拖慢 session;第二条仅对后缀为 Markdown 或 txt 的文件触发,调用一个自定义脚本,检查 em-dash 以及其他"开发者希望在自己的写作里回避"的模式。SessionEnd 向项目日志追加一条记录,给开发者留下跨 session 的一份"自己今天做了什么"的连续流水。流水线不拦截任何动作,不打断 Claude 的节奏,只是悄悄产出备份、应用写作规约、维持日志——一切无须开发者主动介入。

What Pipelines Buy You · 流水线真正"买到"的是什么

The pipeline is small, but it changes the developer's experience of working on the project substantially. She knows that backups exist. She knows that her em-dash policy is being enforced on every edit. She knows that her journal is up to date. The mental load of remembering these things has shifted from her to the pipeline, and the load that has been removed from her is now available for the actual writing work. This is the deeper benefit of pipelines: they take ambient concerns off the developer's mind and handle them as standing background processes, freeing the developer's attention for the work that requires attention.
这条流水线虽小,却显著地改变了开发者在该项目上的工作体验:她知道有备份存在;她知道每次编辑 em-dash 规约都在生效;她知道日志是同步的。"记得这些事"的心智负担,从她身上挪到了流水线上;而腾出来的那份注意力,如今可以投到真正的写作工作上。这正是流水线更深一层的收益——它把"背景里的事"从开发者脑子里搬走,当成"长期运行的后台进程"去处理,把开发者的注意力释放给那些"真正需要注意力"的工作。
A discipline applies to event pipelines that is worth stating directly. Pipelines are powerful, but they are also a form of accumulated complexity, and every hook in a pipeline is a hook the developer is committing to
关于事件流水线,有一条值得直接点出的纪律:流水线很强,但它同时也是"累积复杂度"的一种形态;流水线里的每一条 hook,都是开发者承诺要去维护的——
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 175 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 175
maintain. A small pipeline of three or four well-chosen hooks is a substantial improvement over no pipeline at all. A pipeline of fifteen hooks may be worse than seven, because the failure modes multiply, the cumulative latency adds up, and the developer's ability to debug an unexpected behavior degrades when many hooks are running. The recommendation is to start small, add hooks one at a time, and remove any hook that has not earned its place after a month of use. The pipeline should serve the developer, not the other way around, and a pipeline that has grown into a maintenance burden is a pipeline that has been over-engineered.
一份三四条 hook 精心挑选的小流水线,比"完全没有流水线"已是显著升级;而十五条 hook 的大流水线,可能反而不如七条——因为失效模式成倍增加,累计延迟叠加,多 hook 共同运行时开发者 debug 异常行为的能力也会退化。推荐做法是:小起步、一次加一条、用了一个月还没证明自身价值的 hook 就拿掉。流水线应当服务于开发者,而不是反过来;一条"长成了维护负担"的流水线,就是被过度工程化的流水线。
A second discipline concerns testing. Every hook should be tested before it is committed. The standard testing pattern is to invoke the hook directly from the shell, passing in a representative JSON input on standard input, and observing the exit code and the standard error output. A hook that behaves correctly under direct testing usually behaves correctly inside Claude Code; a hook that does not has a chance of producing inscrutable failures inside the session. The fifteen minutes of direct testing at install time prevents debug sessions later. The pattern is worth adopting unconditionally for any hook that does anything more sophisticated than a one-liner.
第二条纪律关于测试。每条 hook 都应当在"提交"前测过。标准测试模式:在 shell 里直接调用它,在标准输入上喂一段"有代表性"的 JSON 输入,观察退出码和标准错误输出。直接测试下行为正确的 hook,通常在 Claude Code 内也正确;而直接测试下都不对的 hook,进了 session 大概率会吐出莫名其妙的失败。安装时花的十五分钟直测,免去日后的一整轮 debug。这套模式,任何"做的不止一行命令"的事,都值得无条件采用。
A third discipline concerns version control. The .claude directory of a project, including the settings.json file that holds the hook configuration, should be version-controlled alongside the source code. Hooks are part of the project's contract for how work happens in the repository. A team member who clones the project for the first time should receive the entire pipeline automatically, without needing to configure anything herself. The version control discipline ensures this. Pipeline changes should be reviewed in pull requests like any other code change, because a hook change can affect every future session in the project, and the impact of a bad hook can be substantial. The same engineering hygiene that applies to source code applies to hook configuration, and projects that treat their .claude directory with this discipline end up with stable, predictable, well-understood pipelines that genuinely serve the team.
第三条纪律关于版本控制。项目的 .claude 目录(含那份装 hook 配置的 settings.json)应当与源码一起进版本控制。Hook 是项目"工作如何发生"这份契约的一部分。第一次 clone 项目的团队成员,应当自动拿到整条流水线,而不必自己再配置什么——版本控制纪律保证了这一点。流水线的变更,应当像其他代码变更一样走 PR 评审;因为一条 hook 的变更会作用于该项目里之后所有的 session,而一条"坏 hook"的影响可能非常显著。源码适用的那套工程卫生,同样适用于 hook 配置;以这种纪律对待自家 .claude 目录的项目,最终都会得到"稳定、可预测、被充分理解"的流水线,真正服务于团队。

Pipelines and Engineering Practice · 流水线与工程实践

A fourth observation, more philosophical, concerns what pipelines mean for the practice of software engineering. The traditional way of enforcing
第四条观察更偏哲学,关于"流水线"对软件工程实践本身意味着什么。传统上,强制项目规约的方式是——
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 176 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 176
project conventions has been documentation, training, and review. Documentation tells people what the conventions are. Training builds the habits. Review catches violations. Each layer is necessary, and each layer is fallible. A pipeline shifts some of the enforcement from human judgment to mechanism. The conventions that the pipeline enforces no longer need to be documented in a wiki, because they are enforced at the moment they would be violated. The training for new team members shrinks accordingly, because the environment teaches the conventions through its behavior. Review focuses on the things that pipelines cannot enforce, like architectural choices and code quality, rather than on style and formatting issues that mechanism handles cleanly. The result is a team where attention is concentrated on what is genuinely human work, and the mechanical work is done by the mechanism. This is, in the end, the deeper promise of the engineered Claude Code environment, and pipelines are the mechanism that delivers it.
文档、培训、评审。文档把"规约是什么"写下来;培训把习惯建立起来;评审把违规抓出来。每一层都必要,每一层也都靠不住。流水线把一部分强制工作从"人的判断"挪到了"机制"。被流水线强制住的规约,不再需要写到 wiki 里——因为它会在"差点被违反"的那一刻被强制住;对新成员的培训也相应瘦身——因为环境会通过自身的行为"教"出规约;评审则得以聚焦在"流水线兜不住的事"上——比如架构选择、代码质量——而不是把力气花在机制本就能干净处理的风格与格式化上。结果是:整个团队的注意力,被集中到那些真正属于"人"的工作;机械的部分由机制去做。这,正是"工程化的 Claude Code 环境"更深一层的承诺;而流水线,是把这份承诺兑现出来的那条机制。
A final observation about pipelines, before this chapter ends, concerns what happens when several pipelines coexist. A user-level pipeline applies to every project. A project-level pipeline applies inside the specific project. The two pipelines combine: every event that fires runs the hooks from both levels, in a defined order. The combined behavior is the union of the two pipelines, which means that the developer can build a stable personal pipeline at the user level and let projects extend it as needed. A team that has agreed on certain project-level conventions can encode them in the project pipeline. Each developer's personal preferences are layered on top through the user pipeline. The combination is a personalized variant of the team standard, and the developer experiences both layers as a single coherent environment. This layered architecture is one of the quieter strengths of the hook system, and developers who design with it in mind end up with environments that are both standardized and personalized, without conflict between the two.
本章收束前,再补一条关于流水线的观察——关于"多条流水线并存"时会发生什么。用户级流水线作用于所有项目;项目级流水线作用于该项目内部。两者是组合的关系:任一事件触发时,两层级的 hook 都会按既定顺序跑一遍。组合行为就是两层流水线之"并",也就是说:开发者可以在用户层搭一条稳定、长期的个人流水线,再让项目按需扩展它。团队就"项目级规约"达成的共识,被编码进项目流水线;每位开发者的个人偏好,通过用户流水线叠在它上面。组合起来,就是"团队标准的个性化变体";而开发者本人体验到的,是两层合为一块"内洽的环境"。这种"分层"架构,是 hook 系统里相当安静的一项强项;按它来设计的开发者,最终会得到一个"既标准化、又个性化"的环境,两层之间互不冲突。
One more observation deserves a place before this chapter closes, about the felt experience of working with a substantial pipeline installed. The pipeline does its work invisibly. The developer does not see the formatting hook fire, because the file simply ends up formatted. The developer does not see the test guard run, because the tests pass and the session continues normally. The developer does not see the audit logger record her
本章收束前,再放一条观察——关于"装上一条相当规模的流水线之后的工作体感"。流水线做的事是"看不见"的:开发者不会"看见"那条格式化 hook 触发,因为文件"自然而然"就格式化好了;不会"看见"那条测试 guard 跑过,因为测试通过,session 照常推进;也不会"看见"审计 logger 把她的
← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 177 页 · CHAPTER EIGHTEEN 177
commands, because the log file fills silently in the background. What the developer experiences is a working environment that simply behaves the way she wants it to, as if the conventions she cares about were the natural shape of the tool itself. The configuration is invisible, and the behavior is what remains. After a few weeks of working in such an environment, the developer often forgets that the behavior is the result of configuration at all; the environment feels like the default, and the absence of the configuration on a fresh installation feels strange. This is the deepest sign of a successful pipeline. It has receded into the background, and the work has become foreground. The architecture, once built, asks nothing further of the developer, and the developer's attention is free to focus entirely on what she is making.
命令记下来,因为那份日志是在后台默默填满的。开发者真正"体验到"的,是一个"她想要什么它就怎么表现"的工作环境——仿佛她所看重的那些规约,本就是工具自己的天然形状。配置是不可见的,剩下的只有行为。在这样的环境里工作几周之后,开发者常常会忘记:这种行为其实是配置出来的。环境成了"默认",而一个全新安装、没装这套配置的环境,反倒显得别扭。这就是一条成功流水线最深处的标志:它已经退到背景里,工作被推到了前景。这副架构一旦搭好,不再向开发者索取什么;她的注意力,可以完全聚焦于"自己在造的那个东西"。
A reflection before this chapter and this part of the book end. Hooks are quieter than skills and subagents. They do not produce visible artifacts in most sessions. They sit at the edges of the lifecycle, doing their work without drawing attention, and they earn their place by what they prevent rather than by what they produce. The developer who has installed a thoughtful set of hooks may not notice them for weeks at a time, and that invisibility is exactly what good hook 设计 produces. The hooks that are invisible are the hooks that are working. The day comes, eventually, when a hook fires on a real action that needed to be blocked, or a guard catches a problem that would have shipped, or a notifier surfaces an event that needed attention. On that day, the entire investment in the hook bundle is repaid in a single moment, and the developer recognizes, sometimes for the first time, that the architecture has been protecting her all along.
本章、也是本书这一部分收束前的反思:hook 比 skill、subagent 都更"安静"。在大多数 session 里,它们并不产出可见的工件。它们蹲在生命周期的边缘,默默做事,不引人注目;它们赢得位置,靠的是"它们拦下了什么"而非"它们产出了什么"。装上一套"用心挑选过"的 hook 的开发者,可能连续几周都想不起它们;而这种"隐形",恰恰是好 hook 设计所产生的结果。"看不见"的 hook,才是"在工作"的 hook。终有一天,某条 hook 在一次真该拦的动作上跳出来,或者某条 guard 抓住了一个本该被发出去的问题,又或者某条 notifier 把一件确实需要关注的事顶到台面——就在那一天,装这套 hook 套件的全部投入,以一个瞬间被一次性结清;开发者常常是头一次意识到:这套架构,其实一直在保护她。
Part Four ends here. The reader has, over four chapters, learned the mechanics of hooks, internalized the gate-guard-notifier vocabulary that organizes hook 设计, installed a security-hook bundle, and seen how multiple hooks compose into event pipelines that wrap entire workflows in deterministic guarantees. The third pillar of the framework is now in place. Three pillars stand. The next part of the book takes the architecture as a whole and shows how to build a complete environment in which all three pillars work together. Two end-to-end case studies, each substantial, will demonstrate the architecture in concrete form. By the end of the next part, the reader will have seen the framework not as three separate sets of techniques but as a single coherent system, and she will have the blueprint
第四部分至此收束。读者用四章时间,学会了 hook 的机制、内化了"gate-guard-notifier"这套组织 hook 设计的词汇、装好了一套安全 hook 套件,又亲眼见证了多条 hook 是如何组合成事件流水线、把整条工作流包在确定性保证之中。框架的第三根支柱,至此就位。三根支柱,立住了。本书的下一部分,要把架构作为一个整体来谈:如何在"三根支柱一起工作"的前提下,搭建出一套完整的环境。会有两个端到端的、份量扎实的案例研究,把这套架构以具体形式演给你看。读完下一部分,读者将看到的不再是"三套彼此分立的技术",而是"一个内洽的整体系统";她将拿到一份蓝图,

for building such a system in her own work. That synthesis begins in the chapter ahead.
足以在自己的工作里搭出这样一套系统。这场"综合",从下一章开始。

PART FIVE · 第五部分
The Composition Layer: Building a Self-Governing Development Environment · 组合层:搭一个"自我治理"的开发环境

Three pillars, one architecture. The whole begins to mean more than the sum of the parts.
三根支柱,一套架构。整体开始意味着"比各部分之和更多"的东西。

CHAPTER NINETEEN · 第十九章
The Three-Pillar Architecture: Designing a Complete Claude Code System · 三支柱架构:设计一套完整的 Claude Code 系统

← 上一页 下一节 →
原书第 180 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN 180
"Architecture is the art of arranging elements so that the whole has properties none of the elements possess. Without arrangement, there is only inventory." — Christopher Alexander, paraphrased
"所谓架构,就是把元素安排成一种形态,使整体具有任何单一元素都不具备的性质。没有安排,就只剩一份清单。" —— 克里斯托弗·亚历山大(意译)
Up to this point in the book, the three pillars have been treated separately. Skills were the subject of Part Two, subagents the subject of Part Three, hooks the subject of Part Four. Each part developed its pillar in depth, with mechanics, design patterns, and a working library installed by the chapter's end. The treatment was deliberately separate, because each pillar carries its own design discipline and learning each one in isolation is the only way to develop the discipline cleanly. But the separation is now over. The remainder of the book treats the three pillars as components of a single architecture, and this chapter is where the architecture itself gets explicit attention.
到本书此前的位置为止,三根支柱是分开讲的。Skill 是第二部分的主题,subagent 是第三部分的主题,hook 是第四部分的主题。每一部分都把各自那根支柱讲得很深——含机制、设计模式,以及"到章末装好的一套能跑起来的库"。这种分开是有意为之——因为每根支柱都自有一套设计纪律,而"一门门分开学",是把纪律练得干净的唯一办法。但"分开"这件事,到此为止。书剩下的部分,会把三根支柱当作一套架构里的若干组件来对待;而本章,正是让这套架构本身"被显式注意"的地方。
The argument of this chapter is that any sophisticated Claude Code environment uses all three pillars, that the pillars have a natural division of responsibility, that the composition between them follows recognizable patterns, and that the resulting environment has properties that no single pillar could produce on its own. The architecture is not arbitrary. It reflects the structure of the tool, the structure of real engineering work, and the structure of the failure modes that the architecture is built to prevent. Once the reader has internalized the architecture, the design of any new Claude Code environment becomes a matter of applying the same patterns to her specific work, with the patterns themselves as a stable starting point.
本章的论点是:任何一套"够复杂"的 Claude Code 环境,都会同时用上三根支柱;三根支柱之间存在一种自然的职责划分;它们彼此组合时,会遵循若干可识别的模式;而组合出来的环境,具有"任一根支柱单兵作战"都达不到的性质。这套架构并非随意——它反映的是工具本身的结构、真实工程工作的结构、以及"这套架构要去防住"的那几类失效模式的结构。一旦读者把架构内化,设计任何新的 Claude Code 环境,就变成"把这套同样的模式套到自己的具体工作上";模式本身,则成为一个稳定的起点。
Begin with the division of responsibility. Each pillar handles a class of problem that the other two cannot handle as well. Skills handle capability; they teach Claude how to do specific kinds of work consistently. Subagents handle specialization and parallelism; they give specific kinds of work their own focused context, their own tuned role, and the option to run alongside other work simultaneously. Hooks handle determinism; they impose mechanical guarantees on the lifecycle, ensuring that what must happen does happen, and what must not happen cannot happen. The three together cover capability, specialization, and boundary, which are the three categories of concern in any serious workflow.
从"职责划分"讲起。每一根支柱,各自处理一类"另两根处理得没那么好"的问题。Skill 处理"能力"——教 Claude 如何把某类工作一致地做对。Subagent 处理"专门化"与"并行"——为某类工作配上聚焦的上下文、调好的角色,以及"和其他工作并行跑"的可能性。Hook 处理"确定性"——对生命周期施加机械性的保证,让"必须发生的"确实发生,"绝不能发生的"绝不能发生。三者合起来,覆盖了"能力 / 专门化 / 边界"这三类——它们正是任何严肃工作流都绕不开的三个关切类别。
← 上一页 (下一批翻译) →
原书第 181 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 181
A simple way to remember the division is by the question each pillar answers. Skills answer the question, how should this be done. Subagents answer the question, who should do this. Hooks answer the question, what is permitted. A workflow that uses only skills can teach Claude to do good work but has no way to isolate different kinds of work and no way to guarantee enforcement. A workflow that uses only subagents has specialization but no stored knowledge and no enforcement. A workflow that uses only hooks has enforcement but no capability and no specialization. Each pillar, alone, is a partial answer. All three, together, form a complete one.
记住三者分工的一个简单办法,就是看每一柱回答的是哪个问题。Skills 回答的是"这件事该怎么做"。Subagents 回答的是"这件事该由谁做"。Hooks 回答的是"这件事什么被允许做"。只装 skills 的工作流,能教会 Claude 把活做漂亮,但既无法把不同类的工作隔离开来,也无法保证规约被执行。只装 subagents 的工作流,有专门化但没有沉淀下来的知识,也没有强制力。只装 hooks 的工作流,有强制力但没有能力,也没有专门化。任一柱单独存在,都只是一个"局部解";三柱一起,才构成一个完整的答案。

Two Layers of the Architecture · 架构的两层

The next observation, which is more subtle, is that the three pillars sit at different layers of the architecture. Skills and subagents both operate inside the agent loop. They are read by the model. They shape the model's behavior through prose. They live in the probabilistic space where the model interprets, reasons, and decides. Hooks operate outside the loop. They run as shell commands, not as language. They are not interpreted by the model. They are mechanical. The two layers, probabilistic inside and deterministic outside, are the natural partitioning of responsibility in a well-designed environment. The model is given freedom and capability inside the loop. The boundaries of the loop are deterministic. This shape, what one practitioner has called the deterministic outer and the probabilistic inner, is the central architectural pattern of the book, and it is worth holding in working memory because it explains almost every design decision in any complete environment.
下一条观察更微妙一些:三柱其实处在架构的不同层面上。Skills 和 subagents 都是在 agent loop 内部工作的——它们由模型读取,通过对模型吐出的文字(prose)塑形;它们栖身在那个"模型在其中解读、推理、做决定"的概率性空间里。Hooks 则是在 loop 外部工作的——它们以 shell 命令的形式运行,不是语言,也不会被模型"解读";它们是机械的。这两层——内部概率性、外部确定性——正是一个设计良好的环境里"职责"最自然的切分方式。模型在 loop 内被给予自由和能力;loop 的边界则是确定性的。这个形状(有位实践者称之为"外层确定、内层概率")是本书的中心架构范式,值得放在工作记忆里——因为它能解释任何一个完整环境里几乎所有的设计决策。
A worked illustration brings the architecture to life. Consider a developer who needs to perform a code review. The work has several components. The reviewer needs to know how good code reviews are written in this codebase. The reviewer needs to apply different kinds of attention to different aspects of the change. The reviewer needs to be prevented from accidentally approving changes that touch protected paths. Each of these components maps cleanly to one of the three pillars. The how is encoded in skills: a project-level pr-review skill that knows the team's preferred review format. The who is encoded in subagents: parallel security, performance, and architecture specialists who each handle their dimension.
举一个落地的例子,可以让这套架构活起来。想象一位开发者要执行一次 code review。这项工作包含好几个组件:reviewer 需要知道"在这个代码库里,一份好的 code review 应当怎么写";reviewer 需要对变更的不同方面施加不同种类的注意力;reviewer 还需要被防止在"被改的文件刚好落在受保护路径上"时不小心点了 approve。这每一个组件,都能干净地映射到三柱的其中一柱上。"怎么做"编码在 skill 里:一个 project 级别的 pr-review skill,知道本团队偏好的评审格式。"由谁做"编码在 subagent 里:并行运行的安全、性能、架构专员,各自处理自己那一维。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 182 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 182
The what-is-permitted is encoded in hooks: a gate on PreToolUse that blocks any tool call that would mark a PR approved without explicit human confirmation when the PR touches authentication code.
"什么被允许"编码在 hooks 里:一道挂在 PreToolUse 上的 gate,会在"PR 触及身份认证相关代码、且没有任何人工确认时"拦截任何把它标为 approved 的工具调用。
The composition runs naturally. The developer asks for a review. Skills fire because the request matches their descriptions. Subagents are dispatched in parallel because the orchestrator pattern recognizes the multidimensional shape of the work. Hooks watch the lifecycle silently. The review unfolds in two minutes, the unified result is presented to the developer, and the deterministic guarantees protect the boundary that the model alone could not be trusted to respect. None of this required the developer to think about the architecture during the request. She typed a sentence. The architecture did the work.
三柱的组合自然地跑起来。开发者请求一次评审;skill 因为请求匹配了 description 而触发;subagent 因为 orchestrator 范式识别出了"这件事是多个维度"而被并行派出去;hooks 在生命周期里静默地守着。两分钟后,review 跑完,统一的结果呈到开发者面前,确定性的那条边界——模型本身无法被信赖会去守的那条——被 hooks 守住了。整件事,并不要求开发者在请求时心里还惦记着这套架构。她只敲了一句话,架构就替她把活干了。
A second observation about the architecture concerns the order of construction. The natural order in which a developer should build her environment is the order in which the pillars were taught in this book: skills first, subagents second, hooks third. The order is not arbitrary. Skills are the broadest and most generally useful pillar; they pay back immediately in almost every session, and they are the easiest to write well on the first attempt. Subagents are the next layer; they require slightly more design effort but produce dramatic benefits when the work has the right shape. Hooks are the most specialized; their value is concentrated in specific safety-critical concerns and in workflow enforcement, and they require the most careful design to install correctly. Building in this order means that the early investment produces immediate benefits, the middle investment produces architectural benefits, and the late investment produces the deterministic guarantees that complete the system. A developer who tries to build in reverse order, hooks first and skills last, often finds the experience frustrating, because hooks without skills and subagents are just constraints on a vanilla environment, and the constraints feel restrictive rather than enabling.
关于架构的第二条观察,是关于"建造顺序"的。开发者搭建她环境时,自然的顺序,就是本书讲述这三柱的顺序:先 skills,再 subagents,最后 hooks。这个顺序不是随意的。Skills 是覆盖面最广、最普适的那一柱;它几乎在每一个 session 里都能立刻见回报,而且第一次写就写得不错的难度也最低。Subagents 是下一层;它需要稍微多花点设计力气,但只要工作形状对得上,带来的收益是戏剧性的。Hooks 则是最专门化的;它的价值集中在某些"安全攸关"的具体关切,以及工作流强制,装的时候也需要最仔细的设计。按这个顺序搭,意味着前期的投入立刻就见到效益,中期的投入换来架构层面的效益,后期的投入换来"让系统真正闭环"的那些确定性保证。要是反过来——先装 hooks,最后才装 skills——体验常常很令人沮丧:没有 skills 和 subagents 的 hooks,只是套在一个"素面"环境上的约束,这种约束会让人感到被束缚,而不是被赋能。

Failure Modes Prevented · 架构所规避的失效模式

A third observation, this one about the failure modes the architecture prevents. Each pillar prevents a different category of failure. Skills prevent the failure of inconsistency: without skills, the developer's own attention has to carry every convention, and her attention is fallible. Subagents
第三条观察,是关于"这套架构规避了哪些失效模式"。每一柱规避的是不同类别的失效。Skills 规避的是"不一致"这种失效:没有 skills,每一个规约都得靠开发者自己的注意力去扛,而人的注意力是会出错的。Subagents
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 183 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 183
prevent the failure of context pollution: without subagents, the main session's context fills with the residue of every phase of work, and the quality of every phase suffers. Hooks prevent the failure of probabilistic breach: without hooks, the model's judgment is the only line of defense against catastrophic mistakes, and the model's judgment is non-zero failure rate. The architecture, taken as a whole, is a defense in depth against the three most common ways that AI-assisted workflows go wrong. A developer who has installed all three pillars has, in effect, hardened her workflow against the three failure modes that would otherwise haunt it.
规避的是"上下文污染"这种失效:没有 subagents,主会话的上下文会被每一阶段工作的残留物填满,每一阶段的质量都会跟着掉。Hooks 规避的是"概率性破防"这种失效:没有 hooks,模型自己的判断就成了抵御灾难性错误的唯一防线,而模型的判断是有一个非零失败率的。把三柱合起来看,这套架构对"AI 辅助工作流最常见的三种出错方式"形成了一道纵深防御。一个装好了三柱的开发者,事实上是在把她的工作流硬化,以抵御那三种若不这样就会始终缠着她的失效模式。
A fourth observation concerns the design process for adding new capabilities to the environment. When the developer notices a friction in her work that she wants to address, she should ask herself which pillar fits. The classification questions are simple. Is this a knowledge gap, where the developer wishes the environment knew something specific? That is a skill. Is this an attention gap, where the developer wishes a different kind of attention were applied to a kind of work? That is a subagent. Is this a guarantee gap, where the developer wishes that something would always or never happen? That is a hook. Asking the right question first prevents the most common design mistakes, which are building skills that should have been hooks, building hooks that should have been skills, and building subagents that should have been narrow skills inside a regular session. The classification is fast, the right answer is usually obvious once the classification has been made, and the cumulative effect of asking the question every time is an environment whose components live in the right places.
第四条观察,是关于"向环境里加新能力"的设计流程。当开发者在工作中留意到一处她想去消除的摩擦时,她应当先问自己"这该落到哪一柱上"。分类问题本身很简单:这是一个"知识缺口"吗——也就是你希望环境知道某件具体的事?那就是 skill。这是一个"注意力缺口"吗——也就是你希望"某类工作被施加一种不同种类的注意力"?那就是 subagent。这是一个"保证缺口"吗——也就是你希望某件事要么永远发生、要么永远不发生?那就是 hook。先问对问题,可以规避最常见的设计错误:把本该做成 hook 的事做成 skill,把本该做成 skill 的事做成 hook,把本该是"普通 session 里一个窄 skill"的事做成 subagent。分类很快,一旦分类完成,正确答案通常就一目了然;而每次都问一遍这个问题,长年累月下来,环境里的每一个组件都会落在该落的位置上。
A fifth observation concerns over-engineering. The temptation to build an elaborate environment is real, and most developers go through a phase where they install too many skills, too many subagents, too many hooks, and the environment becomes harder to maintain than the work it was supposed to support. The remedy is to start small, add components only when their need is concrete, and remove components that have not been used in a few months. A small, well-designed environment of fifteen skills, six subagents, and eight hooks is dramatically more useful than a sprawling one with fifty of each. The compounding of component count past a certain point becomes a tax rather than a benefit, because the developer has to keep more in her head, the failure modes multiply, and the cost of maintenance
第五条观察是关于"过度工程化"的。搭建一个庞大环境的诱惑是真实的;大多数开发者都会经历这么一个阶段——装了太多 skills、太多 subagents、太多 hooks,环境变得比它本该支持的工作还难维护。解法是:起步要小,只在需求具体且明确时才加新组件,几个月没用上的就清掉。一个小巧、设计良好的环境(15 个 skills、6 个 subagents、8 个 hooks)远比一个松散铺开的 50 个同款有用得多。组件数一旦越过某个临界点,就开始变成"税"而不是"福利"——因为开发者脑子里要装的东西变多,失效模式也成倍增加,而维护的成本,
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 184 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 184
starts to consume the benefit of the components themselves. The discipline of restraint is part of the architecture.
开始反噬组件本身带来的好处。"克制"这条纪律,本身也是这套架构的一部分。
A sixth observation concerns the maintenance posture of the environment. A well-built environment is not a static artifact. It evolves with the developer's work. New skills get added when new frictions emerge. Old skills get refined as the developer's preferences change. Subagents get reshaped as the developer learns which roles benefit most from isolation. Hooks get updated as new threats and conventions emerge. The .claude directory of any active developer should look noticeably different at the end of a year from how it looked at the start, because the developer's environment has been growing alongside her work. The maintenance investment is small; thirty minutes per week is sufficient to keep an environment current and useful. The compounding of that investment, across the years a developer uses her environment, is what produces the gap between developers who treat their tooling seriously and developers who do not.
第六条观察是关于"环境的维护姿态"。一个搭建得好的环境,不是一个静态的产物——它会跟着开发者的工作一起演化。新摩擦出现时,会有新 skill 加进来;开发者偏好变了,旧 skill 会被精修;在实践中摸清"哪些角色最受益于隔离"之后,subagent 会被重新塑形;新的威胁、新的规约浮现时,hook 会被更新。任何一位活跃开发者的 .claude 目录,在一年末尾的样子,都应当和年初的样子明显不同——因为她的环境,一直在和她的工作一起长大。维护的投入并不大:每周 30 分钟,就足以让一个环境保持鲜活、好用。而这份额不大的投入,在开发者使用她环境的这些年里不断复利,正是"认真对待自己工具的开发者"和"不这么做的开发者"之间那条鸿沟的来源。

What This Architecture Means · 这套架构意味着什么

The final observation in this chapter is the one that matters most for the rest of the book. The composition principle, repeated throughout these chapters, is not just a design pattern. It is the difference between using Claude Code and engineering with it. A developer who has built a thoughtful three-pillar environment is doing something fundamentally different from a developer who has not. She is operating inside an environment that has been shaped to her work, that absorbs frictions she would otherwise carry, that protects her from mistakes she would otherwise make, and that produces a level of consistency and quality across her output that no amount of careful prompting alone could match. The investment to build this environment is real but bounded. The benefit, once built, compounds for as long as the developer continues to work in the environment. There is no comparable investment available to a software engineer in 2026 that produces anything close to the leverage of a well-designed Claude Code environment, because no other tool sits at the intersection of capability, specialization, and enforcement that Claude Code does.
本章的最后一条观察,是往后整本书里最关键的一条。在前面这些章节里反复出现的"组合原则",不只是一个设计范式——它是"用 Claude Code"和"用 Claude Code 做工程"之间那条分水岭。一个搭建了深思熟虑的"三柱环境"的开发者,和一个没有这样做的开发者,做着本质不同的事。前者是在一个已经被塑造得贴合她工作的环境里运转:环境吸收她原本要自己扛的摩擦、保护她免于她原本会犯的错、并让她产出的一致性和质量达到"再仔细的 prompt 工程"也匹配不上的水平。搭建这个环境的投入是真实但有限的;一旦搭好,收益会在她继续使用环境的每一天里不断复利。在 2026 年,软件工程师能做的投资里,没有哪一项能在杠杆效率上接近"一个设计良好的 Claude Code 环境"——因为没有任何别的工具,同时坐在能力、专门化、强制这三件事的交汇点上,而 Claude Code 坐在那里。
The next two chapters take the architecture from principle to practice. Each chapter is a substantial case study in which a complete environment is
接下来两章,要把这套架构从原则带到实践。每一章都是一个分量十足的案例研究——会从头到尾为一个特定种类的工作,搭出一个完整的环境——
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 185 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 185
built end-to-end for a specific kind of work. The first case study, in the chapter that follows, is a full-stack development environment for a TypeScript engineer. The second, in the chapter after that, is a solopreneur's environment for a technical founder running product, marketing, and operations from a single Claude Code installation. Together, the two case studies demonstrate the architecture across two very different shapes of work, and they leave the reader with concrete blueprints she can adapt to her own situation. The architectural principles end here. The applied builds begin in the chapter ahead.
端到端地搭出来。第一个案例在紧接的这一章:为一位 TypeScript 工程师搭的全栈开发环境。第二个案例在再后面那一章:为一位独立创业者搭的、用于"从一份 Claude Code 安装里同时跑产品、营销、运营"的环境。两个案例合起来,把同一套架构展示在两种非常不同的工作形状上,也会给读者留下可以拿回去套自己情况的、具体可用的蓝图。架构原则到此为止;实战搭建,从下一章开始。
Before turning to the case studies, a few additional architectural patterns deserve explicit attention, because they show up in almost every well-built environment and the reader should recognize them when she sees them in the chapters ahead.
在转向案例之前,还有几条额外的架构范式值得明说——它们几乎会出现在每一个搭得好的环境里,读者应当能在接下来两章里一看到它们就认出来。
The first pattern is what might be called the project-skill, user-subagent split. In well-designed multi-developer environments, project-specific knowledge tends to live in skills at the project level, while role specialists tend to live as subagents at the user level. The reason is that knowledge is project-specific (a Python project has different conventions from a TypeScript project) but roles are usually personal (a developer's preferred way of approaching code review does not change much when she switches projects). The split allows the developer to maintain a stable personal subagent library that she carries from project to project, while each project contributes its own skills that augment her subagents with project-specific knowledge. The composition pattern from Chapter Fourteen depends on this split working cleanly, and developers who set up their environments with the split in mind from the start avoid a common refactoring later when they realize their subagents have accumulated project-specific knowledge that should have lived elsewhere.
第一条范式,可以叫做"项目级 skill、用户级 subagent"的拆分。在设计良好的多开发者环境里,"项目特有的知识"倾向于作为 skill 落在 project 级别;"角色专家"则倾向于作为 subagent 落在 user 级别。理由在于:知识是项目特有的(Python 项目和 TypeScript 项目的规约不一样),而角色通常是个人的(开发者偏好的 code review 方式,她换了项目也基本不变)。这种切分让开发者可以维护一个稳定的"个人 subagent 库",从项目带到项目;而每个项目自己贡献自己的 skill,在她的 subagent 之上"长出"项目特有的知识。第十四章讲过的"组合范式"依赖这种切分能干干净净地跑;从一开始就按这个切分来组织环境的开发者,会避免掉后来一种很常见的重构:她突然发现,自己的 subagent 不知道什么时候塞进了"本不该属于它"的项目特有知识,得重新拆出来。

Ambient Versus Targeted · 环境级 vs 触发级

The second pattern is the ambient-versus-targeted split, which was introduced in Chapter Two but deserves restatement in the architectural context. Information that is true at all times in a project belongs in CLAUDE.md, where it is loaded into every session's context unconditionally. Information that is true only when a specific kind of work is being done belongs in a skill, where it is loaded only when the skill activates. The line
第二条范式是"环境级 vs 触发级"的切分,第二章引入过,但放在架构的上下文里值得再讲一遍。一个项目里"任何时候都为真"的信息,放在 CLAUDE.md 里——它会在每个 session 的上下文里无条件被加载。"只在做某类特定工作时才为真"的信息,放在 skill 里——只有当这个 skill 被触发时,它才会被加载。这条线
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 186 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 186
between the two is sometimes blurry, but a useful rule is that any piece of knowledge that costs more than fifty words to express, and that is relevant to less than a quarter of the work in the project, almost certainly belongs in a skill rather than in CLAUDE.md. Putting too much in CLAUDE.md inflates the baseline context cost of every session and degrades the model's attention. Putting too little in CLAUDE.md makes the developer brief Claude on the same project facts every session, which defeats the purpose of having ambient context at all. The architectural discipline is to push as much as possible into skills and keep CLAUDE.md to genuinely ambient material.
有时候画得不太清,但有一条好用的经验:任何"表达起来超过 50 个字"、且"相关性覆盖不到项目四分之一工作"的知识块,几乎可以肯定该放在 skill 里,而不是 CLAUDE.md 里。CLAUDE.md 里塞太多,会撑大每个 session 的基线上下文成本,让模型的注意力变差;CLAUDE.md 里塞太少,又会让开发者每个 session 都要把同一组项目事实再向 Claude 复述一遍——这就完全失去了"环境级上下文"存在的意义。架构层面的纪律是:能推到 skill 里的就尽量推到 skill 里,把 CLAUDE.md 留给真正"环境级"的那些内容。
The third pattern is the hooks-as-the-last-resort principle. When the developer is considering whether to enforce some convention with a hook or with a skill, the right default is to start with a skill and only escalate to a hook if the skill is not enough. Skills are softer, more flexible, easier to maintain, and more conversational. Hooks are harder, more rigid, and harder to debug when they go wrong. A convention that the model can reliably honor when reminded should live in a skill. A convention that the model genuinely cannot be trusted to honor, where the consequences of a single failure are substantial, should live in a hook. This default keeps the architecture honest. The developer who reaches for hooks first ends up with a brittle environment that is hard to evolve. The developer who reaches for skills first and escalates to hooks only when necessary ends up with a flexible environment whose enforcement layer is concentrated where it actually earns its place.
第三条范式是"hooks 作为最后手段"原则。当开发者面临"用 hook 还是用 skill 来强制一条规约"的选择时,正确的默认是先 skill,只有当 skill 不够用时才升级到 hook。Skill 更软、更灵活、更易维护、也更像对话;hook 更硬、更刚性、出错时也更难排查。"模型只要被提醒一下,就能稳定地遵守"的规约,应当住在 skill 里;"模型真的不能被信赖去遵守、且一旦失败代价极大"的规约,才应当住到 hook 里。这个默认值守住了架构的诚实。先伸手去抓 hook 的开发者,最后会得到一个脆弱、难以演进的环境;先伸手去抓 skill,只在必要时才升级到 hook 的开发者,会得到一个灵活的环境,其强制层恰好集中在它真正值得出场的地方。
The fourth pattern concerns the lifecycle of any specific configuration entry. Every skill, subagent, and hook the developer adds to her environment goes through a predictable lifecycle. It is created in response to a specific friction. It is used heavily for some period. It either earns its place by being used continuously or fades into disuse. Either way, after roughly six months, the developer should review the entry and ask whether it is still pulling its weight. Entries that have not been used in months should be removed. Entries that have been used heavily but slightly off-fit should be refined. Entries that have been used as a starting point for several variants should perhaps be split into more specific siblings. This kind of pruning maintenance is what keeps an environment from accumulating dead weight, and the developers who treat their .claude directories as living
第四条范式是关于"任意一条配置项的生命周期"的。开发者每加进环境里一个 skill、一个 subagent、一个 hook,都会走一条可预测的生命周期:因某个具体摩擦而被创建;有一段时间被频繁使用;之后要么靠持续被使用而"赢得自己的位置",要么慢慢淡出。不管哪种,大概 6 个月后,开发者都应当回头 review 一下这条项,问问"它还在撑着场面吗"。几个月没被用过的项,删掉;被频繁用但用得有点别扭的,精修;已经被当作多个变体起点的,考虑拆成几个更专门的兄弟项。这种"剪枝式"的维护,正是让一个环境不至于积累"死重"的关键——而那些把 .claude 目录当"活的工件"对待、
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 187 页 · CHAPTER NINETEEN(续) 187
projects, gardened regularly, end up with environments that stay sharp for years rather than gradually decaying into clutter.
像对待项目一样、定期打理它的开发者,他们的环境会保持锋利好些年,而不是慢慢腐烂成一堆乱糟糟的东西。
The fifth pattern, and the last to name explicitly before the case studies, is the small-and-good principle. A small environment of fifteen well-designed components beats a large environment of fifty mediocre ones, almost always. The temptation to build large is real, especially after the first few components produce visible benefits. The discipline is to resist building large for its own sake, and to add components only when their need is concrete and undeniable. Many of the most effective environments in practice contain fewer than thirty components total. Their effectiveness comes from how well-tuned the components are, not from their count. The reader who internalizes the small-and-good principle will produce environments that work beautifully and remain maintainable. The reader who does not will produce environments that impress at first glance and frustrate at the first attempt to evolve them six months later.
第五条范式,也是进入案例前最后要明说的,叫"小而精"原则。一个 15 个组件、个个设计得好的小型环境,几乎在所有情形下都胜过 50 个组件、但个个平庸的大型环境。诱惑是真实存在的——尤其是前几个组件带来了可见收益之后,人会很容易飘起来。要守的纪律是:不为"大"而大,只在需求具体且不可否认时才加新组件。实践中很多最有效的环境,总数都不到 30 个。这些环境之所以有效,靠的是每个组件都被调得有多好,而不是组件的数量。把"小而精"原则真正吃进去的读者,会造出用起来漂亮、维护起来也轻的环境;吃不进去的,会造出"乍一看令人印象深刻、但六个月后一要演进就让人抓狂"的环境。
A final architectural observation, before the case studies begin, concerns the relationship between the three-pillar architecture and the deeper craft of software engineering. Engineers who have worked on production systems for years recognize the architecture in this book as familiar. Skills resemble the configuration files that have always shaped good engineering systems. Subagents resemble the worker pools and microservice patterns that decompose monolithic systems into focused specialists. Hooks resemble the middleware, the policy enforcement points, and the observability layers that wrap deterministic guarantees around probabilistic services. The three-pillar architecture is not, in its bones, a new invention. It is the application of well-established software-architecture patterns to the specific problem of working with an AI assistant. This continuity is reassuring, because it means that the discipline this book asks the reader to develop is the same discipline that produces good software in general. A developer who builds her Claude Code environment well becomes a slightly better software engineer along the way, because the patterns she practices on her environment are the same patterns she should practice on her production systems. The book's teaching, in this sense, transfers beyond the tool.
进入案例之前,最后一条架构观察,是关于"三柱架构"和"软件工程这门更深的手艺"之间关系的。在生产系统上工作过多年的工程师,会在本书这套架构里认出自己的老熟人。Skills 像那些一直以来"塑造优秀工程系统"的配置文件;subagents 像把单体系统拆成专门化"小而专"的 worker pool 和微服务范式;hooks 像那些"把确定性保证包在概率性服务外头"的中间件、策略执行点和可观测性层。三柱架构骨子里并不是什么新发明;它是把已经成熟的软件架构范式,应用到了"和 AI 助手协作"这个具体问题上。这种连续性让人安心——它意味着本书让读者培养的纪律,和"做好的软件"这件事本身所要求的纪律,其实是同一种。开发者把 Claude Code 环境搭好,顺带会成为一个稍微好一点的软件工程师,因为她在环境上练的这些范式,正是她应该在生产系统上也练的那些。从这个意义上,本书所教的东西,溢出了工具本身。
← 上一节 Chapter 20: Full-Stack Case Study →
原书第 188 页 · CHAPTER TWENTY 188

CHAPTER TWENTY · 第二十章
Case Study: Building a Full-Stack Development Environment
案例研究:搭建一个全栈开发环境

"The proof of the architecture is in the building. Until something stands, the design is only an opinion." — a structural engineer, anonymous
"架构是否成立,要在盖起来之后才算数。在有什么东西真正立在那儿之前,设计只是意见。" —— 一位结构工程师(匿名)
This chapter is a build. The reader will follow, end to end, the construction of a complete Claude Code environment for a single specific kind of work: full-stack web development in TypeScript, with React on the front end and Node with PostgreSQL on the back end. The reader does not need to be a TypeScript developer for the chapter to be useful. The point of the case study is the shape of the construction, not the specific technologies. A reader who works in a different stack can read the chapter as a template and substitute her own languages, her own frameworks, her own conventions. The skeleton is what matters. The flesh is illustrative.
这一章就是一场"搭"。读者会从头到尾跟着,把一份完整的 Claude Code 环境搭出来——针对一种非常具体的工作:TypeScript 全栈 Web 开发,前端 React,后端 Node + PostgreSQL。读者本人并不需要是 TypeScript 开发者,这一章才有价值。这个案例研究的重点,是"搭建的形状",而不是具体技术栈。读者在用别的技术栈,完全可以把这一章当作一个模板来读,把语言、框架、规约换成自己的。骨架才是重点;血肉只是示例。
The developer in this case study is a senior engineer at a small product company. She works in a single repository that contains a Next.js front end, a Node API server, a PostgreSQL database with Prisma as the ORM, a test suite written in Vitest, and deployment configuration in Docker and Kubernetes. The team has agreed conventions for how new features are scaffolded, how tests are written, how database migrations are produced, how PR descriptions are written, and how releases are tagged. The conventions are presently maintained in a wiki that nobody reads after their first week on the team. The developer wants to encode the conventions into her Claude Code environment so that the environment enforces and applies them automatically, freeing her from having to remember them and freeing her teammates from having to be reminded of them in every code review.
案例里的这位开发者,是某小型产品公司的一位资深工程师。她在单一仓库里工作:Next.js 前端、Node API 服务、用 Prisma 作为 ORM 的 PostgreSQL 数据库、用 Vitest 写的测试套件、Docker + Kubernetes 的部署配置。团队已经就一些规约达成了一致:新 feature 怎么搭脚手架、测试怎么写、数据库 migration 怎么生成、PR description 怎么写、release 怎么打 tag。这些规约目前被维护在一个 wiki 里——而入职第一周之后,没人再去看那个 wiki。这位开发者希望把规约编码进她的 Claude Code 环境,让环境自己去强制、自己去应用,免得她每次都要去记、也免得队友每次在 code review 里被反复提醒。
The build will produce, in the project's .claude directory, a configuration that includes eight skills, four subagents, and six hooks. By the end of the chapter, the directory will be committed to the repository, every developer who clones the project will inherit the environment
这次搭建会在项目里的 .claude 目录下,产出一份包含 8 个 skills、4 个 subagents、6 个 hooks 的配置。等这一章结束时,这个目录会被提交进仓库;之后凡是 clone 这个项目的开发者,都会自动继承这一环境——
← Chapter 19 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 189 页 · CHAPTER TWENTY(续) 189
automatically, and the daily experience of working in the project will be substantially different from what it was before the build began.
在这个项目里"日常工作的体验",会和搭建开始之前有明显不同。
Begin with the directory structure. The .claude directory at the project root is organized as follows.
先从目录结构开始。项目根目录下的 .claude 长这样:
.claude/
├── settings.json
├── skills/
│   ├── component-scaffold/
│   ├── api-route-scaffold/
│   ├── prisma-migration/
│   ├── pr-description/
│   ├── commit-message/
│   ├── test-conventions/
│   ├── api-doc/
│   └── error-format/
├── agents/
│   ├── security-auditor.md
│   ├── performance-reviewer.md
│   ├── test-writer.md
│   └── migration-planner.md
└── hooks/
    ├── block-dangerous.sh
    ├── protect-files.sh
    ├── format-on-save.sh
    └── tests-must-pass.sh
Each of the components has a specific job in the larger system. The skills encode the project's knowledge. The subagents provide specialized roles for the major review and authoring tasks. The hooks enforce the deterministic guarantees. Together, the configuration makes a substantial development workflow possible to perform in a single Claude Code session, with the environment carrying most of the load that the developer used to carry herself.
每一个组件,都在这套大系统里承担着特定职责。Skills 编码的是项目里的知识;subagents 为"主要的 review 与撰写任务"提供专门化角色;hooks 强制的是确定性保证。三者合起来,使得"在一次 Claude Code 会话里完成一份相当分量的开发工作流"成为可能——原本由开发者自己扛的那一大部分负担,现在由环境接管了。
Begin building with the skills, because skills are the foundation. The first skill is component-scaffold, which generates a new React component in the project's conventional layout. A React component in this codebase is a folder containing the component file, a test file, a Storybook story, and an index re-export. The folder convention is that each component lives at src/components/[ComponentName]/, with PascalCase naming throughout. The skill captures this convention and the templates for each generated file.
先从 skills 搭起——skills 是地基。第一个 skill 是 component-scaffold,作用是按项目的规约布局生成一个新的 React 组件。这个代码库里,一个 React 组件是一个目录,里面装:组件文件本身、一份测试文件、一份 Storybook story、以及一个 index 的再导出。规约上的目录约定是:每个组件住在 src/components/[ComponentName]/ 下,全程 PascalCase 命名。这个 skill 把这条规约和每个被生成文件的模板一起,都收进了它的定义里。
← 上一节 下一节 →
原书第 190 页 · CHAPTER TWENTY(续) 190
The second skill is api-route-scaffold, which generates a new API route in the Next.js conventional layout, with the route handler, the validation schema, the test file, and the OpenAPI documentation fragment. The skill captures the team's convention that every route has explicit validation using Zod, every route has a test that exercises the happy path and at least two error cases, and every route has a corresponding OpenAPI fragment that lives alongside the route file.
第二个 skill 是 api-route-scaffold,作用是按 Next.js 的规约布局生成一条新的 API 路由:包含路由 handler、validation schema、测试文件、以及 OpenAPI 文档片段。这个 skill 把团队规约也一起收进去了:每条路由必须用 Zod 做显式校验;每条路由的测试必须覆盖 happy path 加至少两种错误情形;每条路由必须有一份与之同址存放的 OpenAPI 片段。

The Component Scaffold Skill · 组件脚手架 skill

---
name: component-scaffold
description: Scaffolds a new React component in the project's conventional layout, complete with test file, Storybook story, and index export. Use whenever the user asks to "create a new component," "scaffold a component called X," "set up a React component," or describes wanting a new component file structure created.

# Component Scaffold

When asked to scaffold a component, follow this procedure.

1. Get the component name from the user. Convert to PascalCase if needed.
2. Create the folder at `src/components/[ComponentName]/`.
3. Create the four files using the templates in `assets/`:
   - `[ComponentName]/index.ts`, re-exports the component as a named export
   - `[ComponentName]/[ComponentName].tsx`, the component itself
   - `[ComponentName]/[ComponentName].test.tsx`, Vitest test file
   - `[ComponentName]/[ComponentName].stories.tsx`, Storybook story
4. Substitute the placeholder `__COMPONENT_NAME__` with the actual name in each file.
5. Confirm by listing the four files created.

Use functional components with hooks. Use TypeScript strict mode. Define props as a named interface above the component. Use CSS modules for styling, with a matching .module.css file.

The Migration Skill · Migration skill

The third skill is prisma-migration, which produces a Prisma migration file from a description of the schema change, with the up migration, the down migration, and a note about whether the change is backward-compatible.
第三个 skill 是 prisma-migration,作用是:给定一段对 schema 变更的描述,产出一份 Prisma migration 文件——包含 up migration、down migration、以及一条关于"这次变更是否向后兼容"的说明。

API Route Scaffold(续) · API 路由脚手架(续)

When asked to scaffold an API route, follow this procedure.

1. Get the route path and HTTP method from the user.
2. Create the route handler at `app/api/[path]/route.ts`.
3. Create the validation schema at `app/api/[path]/schema.ts`. All input must be validated with Zod before being processed.
4. Create the test file at `app/api/[path]/route.test.ts`. Cover happy path, validation failure, and one business-logic failure.
5. Create the OpenAPI fragment at `app/api/[path]/openapi.yaml`. Document the request shape, the response shape, and all error codes.
6. Wire the route into the API surface following the project's convention.

Every route must validate its input with Zod. Every route must produce errors in the project's standard error format (see the error-format skill). Every route must have a test file with at least three tests.

Prisma Migration Skill · Prisma Migration skill

---
name: prisma-migration
description: Produces a Prisma migration from a described schema change, with up and down migrations and a backward-compatibility note. Use whenever the user asks to "create a migration," "add a migration for X," "I need to change the schema," or describes wanting a database schema change made.
allowed-tools: Read, Write, Bash
---
# Prisma Migration

When asked to create a migration, follow this procedure.

1. Read the current schema at `prisma/schema.prisma`.
2. Apply the change to the schema. Be conservative: prefer additive changes (new columns, new tables) over destructive ones (dropped columns, renamed tables) whenever possible.
3. Generate the migration file using `npx prisma migrate dev --create-only --name [descriptive_name]`.
4. Read the generated migration. Verify it does what was intended.
5. If the migration is destructive, write a note at the top of the migration file explaining what is being changed and what the rollback strategy is.
6. Report:
   - What changed
注:以上是 prisma-migration skill 的核心定义,body 的第 6 步"报告什么变了"在下一页继续展开。
注:以上是 prisma-migration skill 的核心定义,body 的第 6 步"报告什么变了"在下一页继续展开。

   - Whether a data migration is needed in addition to the schema migration

PR Description Skill · PR Description skill

---
name: pr-description
description: Drafts a pull request description in the team's standard format. Use whenever the user asks for a PR description, pull request description, PR body, or asks "write a PR for this branch," "make me a PR description." This skill is for drafting the description of a PR you are creating, not for reviewing one.
allowed-tools: Bash, Read, Grep
---
# PR Description

When asked for a PR description, follow this procedure.

1. Identify the diff. Run `git log` and `git diff` against the merge base on main.
2. Read the diff in full.
3. Produce the description in this exact format:

## Summary
A single sentence in the imperative mood describing the change.

## What changed
A bulleted list of concrete changes, one bullet per logical unit.

## Schema changes
If the diff includes any Prisma migrations, summarize the schema changes here. State whether they are backward-compatible. Omit this section if there are no migrations.

## Testing
Describe how the change was tested. List any new test files created.

## Risk
One sentence categorizing risk as low, medium, or high with a one-line reason.
注:上面这段是 pr-description skill 的核心定义,body 中"Deploy notes"小节将在下一页继续。

Test Conventions Skill · Test Conventions skill

---
name: test-conventions
description: Encodes this project's test conventions, including framework choice, file layout, mocking patterns, and naming. Activated by the test-writer subagent and by the user when explicitly asked about testing conventions.
allowed-tools: Read
---
# Test Conventions

This project uses Vitest. Test files are colocated with the file under test, with a `.test.ts` or `.test.tsx` suffix.

## Structure
- One `describe` block per public function or component.
- One `it` block per scenario. Every public function should have at minimum: happy path, one boundary, one error.
- Use `beforeEach` for setup that resets state. Avoid `beforeAll`.

## Mocking
- Use Vitest's `vi.mock` for module mocks.
- Use MSW (Mock Service Worker) for HTTP mocks. Set up handlers in the test file's `beforeAll`.
- Never make real network calls in tests.
- Never write to the real file system in tests. Use `memfs` if file operations are needed.
注:上面这段是 test-conventions skill 的核心定义,剩余的"## Database"、"## Async"、"## Naming"三个小节将在下一页继续。
注:上面这段是 test-conventions skill 的核心定义,剩余的"## Database"、"## Async"、"## Naming"三个小节将在下一页继续。
← 上一节 下一节 →

The Test Writer Subagent · Test Writer subagent

The third subagent is test-writer, customized slightly for this project. The customization is in the description, which now mentions Vitest and the project's specific test conventions, and in the system prompt, which directs the subagent to load the test-conventions skill at the start of its work.
第三个 subagent 是 test-writer,针对本项目做了轻微定制:定制落在两处——description 中点名了 Vitest 和本项目特有的测试规约;system prompt 中指示这个 subagent 一开工就先把 test-conventions skill 加载起来。

Test Conventions(续) · Test Conventions(续)

## Database
- Tests that touch the database use a transaction that is rolled back after the test.
- Never use the production or staging database for tests.

## Async
- Always await async operations explicitly. Do not rely on returned promises being awaited by the test runner.
- Use `vi.useFakeTimers()` for tests that depend on time.

## Naming
- Test files: `[FileName].test.ts` or `[ComponentName].test.tsx`.
- Test descriptions: imperative, e.g. "creates a user when given valid input," not "should create a user."

Test Writer Subagent · Test Writer subagent 定义

---
name: test-writer
description: Writes Vitest test files for a given function or module, following this project's test conventions. Use whenever the user asks for tests, says "add coverage," "write a test for this," or finishes a feature without tests in place.
tools: Read, Write, Bash, Glob
model: sonnet
---

You are a senior test engineer. Your job is to write tests that catch real bugs, not tests that only exercise the happy path so that coverage numbers look better.

Your method:

1. Activate the project's test-conventions skill at the start of your work.
2. Read the file under test and any closely related files needed to understand its behavior.
3. Identify the public surface of the file: functions, methods, classes that other code calls.
4. For each item on the public surface, write tests covering at minimum:
   - The happy path
   - At least one boundary condition
   - At least one error or invalid-input case
5. Mock external dependencies using the project's mocking patterns (Vitest `vi.mock` for modules, MSW for HTTP).
6. Place test files alongside the code under test, following the project's naming convention.

What you do not do:
- You do not modify the code under test, even if you discover bugs while writing tests. Note the bug as a comment in the test and flag it in your final summary.
- You do not write tests that only call the function and assert that it does not throw.
- You do not mock the function under test. You mock its dependencies.
- You do not produce excess of nearly-identical tests. Three meaningful tests beat ten redundant ones.

End with a list of the test files you wrote and any bugs or concerns you noticed in the code under test.
注:以上是 test-writer subagent 的完整定义。tools 字段限定它可用的工具集(本案例中拿不到 WebFetch);model: sonnet 指明它跑在 Sonnet 模型上;System prompt 用了"方法步骤 + 显式禁止项"的双层结构,以避免它写出"只验证函数不抛异常"那种空壳测试。
注:以上是 test-writer subagent 的完整定义。tools 字段限定它可用的工具集(本案例中拿不到 WebFetch);model: sonnet 指明它跑在 Sonnet 模型上;System prompt 用了"方法步骤 + 显式禁止项"的双层结构,以避免它写出"只验证函数不抛异常"那种空壳测试。

注:这段 JSON 是 settings.json 的开头。完整结构由四个事件构成:SessionStart(打 git 状态)、PreToolUse(两道门:block-dangerous.sh 给 Bash、protect-files.sh 给文件编辑)、PostToolUse(运行格式化与测试)、Stop(检查未提交变更)。余下内容在下一页继续。
